# Approved compatibility departures

The [compatibility policy](compatibility.md) requires approval of named outcomes. The live review on **2026-09-27** approved the document, timing, transaction, lifecycle and short-hotkey-file outcomes below. Their corrected behavior becomes default when implemented and validated. No row is implemented or measured here; approval is not a fixture pass.

The suffixes refine the broader candidate rows so approval cannot be mistaken for acceptance of unrelated changes in the same row. Evidence remains pinned in the [candidate ledger](proposals/compatibility-policy.md), [document-model proposal](proposals/document-model.md) and [time-semantics proposal](proposals/time-semantics.md).

| Stable entry | Old evidence / trigger | Approved outcome | Approval scope and required validation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C03-preservation | Unknown ASS sections may lose structure or move colon records into Script Info. | Retain unknown sections and unchanged raw content when saving; editing a Line must not discard unrelated unknown payload. | User: “Approve these three outcomes” in the document batch. Fixture ordered/repeated sections, embedded payloads, encoding and changed/unchanged records. This does not approve replacing all positional parsing or normalizing SSA semantics. |
| C02-loss-preview | Format conversion can remove tags/styles/groups/records or synthesize timing without the proposed revision-bound loss review. | Preview conversion losses, require explicit acceptance of the listed losses, and retain the source Document. A changed document/options invalidates the plan. | Same document-batch approval. Validate text role, removed/normalized content, cancellations and stale plans. This does not approve every underlying conversion/parser correction, or the detailed preview UI. |
| C05-audio-association | A Session's later document can inherit Audio from the preceding entry when its Audio field is omitted. | Resolve audio from that document's own media context or none; never inherit it from a preceding tab. | Same document-batch approval. Test two tabs with Audio only on the first, explicit second associations and missing media; originals stay unchanged. Catalog/recovery behavior is not otherwise changed. |
| C01-equality | Equal-time `>=` and `<=` return false. | Equal values satisfy both comparisons. | User: “Approve these three timing fixes” in the timing batch. Audit all callers; retain old/new boundary fixtures. Other rounding and clamp behavior is not approved by this entry. |
| C01-fps-isolation | MicroDVD interpretation can use shared/default FPS state and fallback behavior. | Use an explicit per-Document rational MicroDVD FPS; preserve raw frame integers while rate is unknown. One document's rate cannot reinterpret another's frames. | Same timing-batch approval. Test two distinct rates, unknown rate and explicit media/rate selection. Do not silently assign a new guessed rate or rewrite existing authored time values. |
| T42-A | Audio frame alignment treats `target − anchor` as an absolute timestamp before mapping to a frame. | Compute the alignment offset by subtracting target and anchor frame indices under the same explicit timebase. | Same timing-batch approval. For frame starts 0, 40, 81, 120 ms and anchor 40 → target 81 ms, the offset is 1 rather than 2. Test CFR/VFR, origins, boundaries and negative offsets. |
| C07-atomic-rejection | A command may mutate state before a late rejection; source-backed examples are in the [transaction proposal](proposals/edit-transactions.md). | Rejected commands leave content, selection, dirty state and history unchanged. | User: “Approve both outcomes” for the named transaction batch. Validate rejection before/after validation, protected/stale targets and publication failure. This does not change Lua validation, undo stubs or external script effects. |
| C43-save-identity | History pruning/branching can lose the saved index. | Preserve a durable saved-content identity independently of history capacity/branching; pending drafts still count as unsaved work. | Same transaction-batch approval. Verify prune, redo branch, return to saved content, pending drafts and asynchronous save revisions. Draft navigation/Save inclusion and other undo policies remain undecided. |
| L58-write-close | Source observations show cancellation/write-result propagation risks; see [lifecycle proposal](proposals/application-lifecycle.md). | A Save-dependent close proceeds only after confirmed successful writing. | User: “Approve all three outcomes” for the named lifecycle batch. Test cancel, short write, disk full, replace failure and newer edits after authorization. Explicit discard remains a separate choice; no automatic draft commit. |
| L58-staged-replacement | A failed/cancelled open or reload can risk the current work. | Retain existing work until a replacement has successfully staged and its activation is authorized. | Same lifecycle-batch approval. Verify parse/resource failure, cancellation, stale target revisions and changed replacement options. No automatic overwrite or blanket parser correction. |
| L58-recovery-copy | Recovery source and current destination must remain independently recoverable. | Restore into a separate unsaved copy, with drafts separate and original files untouched. | Same lifecycle-batch approval. Test committed/draft separation, corrupt snapshots, repeated restore and original hashes. Retention, cleanup and automatic draft publication are not approved by this outcome. |
| C04-short-file | Legacy hotkey loading can replace a short valid file with defaults based on record count. | Import its valid bindings regardless of small record count; preserve unknown/invalid records as visibly unresolved. | User: “Approve C04-short-file” on 2026-09-27. See [import contract](proposals/settings-import.md). Verify small/empty/malformed/mixed files, source preservation and repeat import. This does not approve changed shortcut precedence or reinterpret invalid entries as deliberate unbinding. |
| G56-contiguity | Structural edits can orphan, split or misassociate flag-based Line-group runs; ordinary duplication inside a valid run is not a break. | Allow edits that retain valid groups; reject an actual group-breaking result before mutation and offer Cancel or explicit Remove group before a separately reviewed retry. Removal visibly deletes its description Line. | User: “Reject; offer explicit removal” after the [Grid companion](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/blob/4b1631c2e7c2ab5c58d899e7ae098021d0e1766b/docs/prototypes/grid-boundary-review-notes.md). Validate insert/duplicate/split/move/sort/delete/create across group boundaries, unchanged state on rejection, explicit removal loss and Undo. No automatic splitting, flag removal, description preservation-as-comment or mandatory review for every command is approved. |
| J56-selected-only-join | With A/B/C selected in Document order A,B,X,C, source Join deletes B/X/C while taking text only from A/B/C; unselected X can disappear. | Join removes only selected contributors that are absorbed into the retained Line; unselected intervening Lines keep their content and timing. | User: “Preserve unselected Lines” after the same companion. Validate both Join variants, hidden/unselected rows and the two-selected A,X,B control where X already survives. This changes the deletion set only, not unrelated retained-field, timing, translation, draft or group rules. Source inference is not an executed legacy GUI result. |
| T49-srt-final-cue | Source reading suggests saving some SRT files can drop the final cue. | **Conditional:** if a fixture reproduces the drop, the rewrite writes the final cue. If it does not reproduce, nothing changes. | User: "Fix it if confirmed", 2026-09-29, [translation surface](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/49). Characterize first; record old/new bytes and the exact trigger. |
| S57-rule-offsets | Sequential correction rules may apply with stale offsets after an earlier rule changes length. | **Conditional:** each rule applies against the text produced by the previous rule. | User: "Fix both if confirmed", 2026-09-29, [search surface](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/57). Reproduce with length-changing rule pairs before changing anything. |
| S57-from-selected | "From selected" eligibility is wrong in some cases. | **Conditional:** the search starts at the selected Line and includes it as documented. | Same approval. Characterize the exact misbehaving cases first; unaffected cases stay identical. |
| C82-srt-blank-break | [Legacy capture 37011681370](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/actions/runs/37011681370): in a CRLF SRT file, a cue followed by a blank line gains a trailing `\N` (the blank line is a `"\r"` token), so saving adds an extra blank line. | A blank or whitespace-only line adds nothing to the cue text, with the same rule for LF and CRLF. | User: "fix them all", 2026-10-02, after the C4 candidate defects were reported. Test CRLF and LF blank separators, whitespace-only lines, the final cue and cue-number trimming; unchanged files still save byte for byte. |
| C87-unconfirmed-roundtrip | [Legacy capture 37014681128](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/actions/runs/37014681128): the legacy save writes an Unconfirmed TLMode original with the effect form-feed+D, but its loader trims the form feed away before comparing, so Unconfirmed is never read back. | An original whose Effect field is exactly form-feed+D loads as Unconfirmed. | Same approval. Test load, save, reload round-trip, and that other effects (including a bare `D`) stay confirmed. |
| C87-nondialogue-partner | Same capture: when a `;` comment (or a lone `{...}` line) follows a TLMode original, the legacy loader takes it as the translation, then saves the original's text twice and loses the comment. | Such a line is not taken as the translation. It stays a comment in place, and the original stays an unpaired Line with its own fields. | Same approval. Test `;` and `{...}` partners in LF and CRLF files, the save of the edited original, and reload stability. Other unreadable partners keep the characterized legacy pairing. |
| E63-invalid-commit | End before Start or an invalid margin is corrected or accepted by the legacy editor (to characterize). | Commit is blocked with an inline error by default. A preference restores the legacy behavior. | User: "block by default but users can change the behavior to old", 2026-09-29, [Line editor surface](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/63). The legacy path stays implemented and tested. |
| E63-comment-original | The legacy "Hide original" command wraps the original text in braces. | Behavior unchanged; renamed **Comment out original**. The action keeps its legacy ID alias for the importer. | Naming only, same approval. |
| A33-depctrl-linux | The legacy Windows package ships DependencyControl's native modules (BadMutex, PreciseTimer, DownloadManager); the legacy Linux package does not, so DependencyControl and every script that requires it fail to load on Linux. | Build and ship the three modules from the unchanged `Thirdparty/DependencyControl` sources on Windows and Linux, so DependencyControl loads on both. | User, 2026-10-03: "ship them too", answering the L6 report that the rewrite builds them on Linux as well. Validation: the L6 corpus loads the DependencyControl Toolbox on both platforms; packaging (Phase 5) must place them under Include/BM, PT and DM. Windows behaviour is unchanged by this entry. |
| P1-remove-untitled | Legacy "Remove subtitles from the editor" on an Untitled Document asks to save and then leaves the Document as it is (it only clears a tab that has a file). | Remove subtitles (P1's New) replaces the editing target with the default Untitled Document whether or not it had a file, after the close review. | User, 2026-10-04: "the new behavior is ok", answering the P7 report. Validation: `close_review_tests` New cases; legacy's no-op for Untitled Documents is not reproduced. |
| F2-action-step | With "Add to selection" or "Deselect" and no Line matching, legacy still applies the action (Cut, Delete, Move, Set as comment) to the earlier selection but records no history step; the change joins the next step. | The action is its own "Selecting lines" step. | User, 2026-10-04: "yes", answering the F2 report. Validation: `select_lines_tests` AnActionOnAnEarlierSelectionIsOneStepEvenWithoutMatches. |
| F2-move-hidden | Select lines' Move to beginning/end deletes every selected Line but inserts copies only of the Lines its walk saw, so hidden Lines selected earlier are lost. | Move takes every selected Line, hidden ones included, in Document order, keeping LineIds; they stay selected. | User, 2026-10-04: "fix them". Validation: `select_lines_tests` MoveTakesHiddenSelectedLinesToo. |
| F2-style-cancel | Cancel in the Select dialog's "Choose styles" still fills the search with "^$" and switches to Styles and regular expressions. | Cancel changes nothing; OK fills the pattern as before. | User, 2026-10-04: "fix them". Validation: `shell_tests` selectLinesDialogSelectsAndActs. |
| G6-sort-effect | Legacy sorteffect compares the Actors when the Effects differ, so Sort by Effect orders by Actor. | Sort by Effect orders by Effect (locale collation), ties by Start. | User, 2026-10-04: "fix them or keep the rewrite behaviors" for the open questions. Validation: `grid_commands_tests` SortLines.EveryKeyFollowsTheLegacyComparators. |
| G8-block-step | Opening or closing a hidden block changes visibility without a history step; legacy records it with the next change. | Each open or close is its own "Filtering" step. | Same 2026-10-04 answer (the rewrite's behaviour kept). Validation: `grid_filtering_tests`. |
| E1-nonass-font | For SRT and MicroDVD the font dialog and colour picker pass PutinNonass its arguments swapped, so one Line gets the regex text ({f:([^}]*)}, {C:([^}]*)}); a size change writes the font name; bold/italic write <Y:b>/<Y:i> into SRT and underline/strikeout ASS \u/\s tags into every line format. | MicroDVD gets {F:name}, {S:size}, {C:BBGGRR}, {Y:b}/{Y:i} (replacing the tag before the caret); SRT gets the editor buttons' <b> <i> <u> <s> and is left unchanged by font name, size and colour (it has no such markup); MicroDVD underline/strikeout, MPL2 and TMPlayer are unchanged, as the buttons leave them. | Same 2026-10-04 answer. Validation: `editor_font_colour_tests` LineFormatsWriteTheFontValues and EditorColour.LineFormats. |
| A33-depctrl-toolbox | The legacy automation capture reports the DependencyControl Toolbox failing to load (L6 candidate difference). | The rewrite loads it (with A33-depctrl-linux's modules); the difference is accepted, not reproduced. | Same 2026-10-04 answer. Validation: the L6 corpus capture. |
| A33-dialog-deadlock | The legacy app at 20d647c4 (Windows release v0.0.1-rc.1 and the Linux build) deadlocks on every automation macro that shows a dialog: LuaCommand::Run waits for the script thread with wxThread::Wait (wx 3.3.3: a blocking wait that pumps no messages), while LuaProgressSink::LuaDisplayDialog waits for the main thread to show the dialog; aegisub.dialog.open and aegisub.dialog.save share that path. Captured with minidump stacks (tools/legacy-capture/captures/windows-20d647c4). | The rewrite shows the dialog and returns its answer (and the open/save dialogs' paths) to the script. | User, 2026-10-04: "Approve under R3", answering the S3 capture; L2 #120 closes on the legacy source characterization since legacy can show no script dialog. Validation: AutomationDialogTests::captureProbeDialogCasesAnswer on Linux and Windows. |
| L6-lua52-linux | The legacy Linux build linked the distribution's LuaJIT without the Lua 5.2 extensions (no table.pack, ipairs ignoring __ipairs), so scripts built on Functional or ASSFoundation (all line0 scripts, many others) failed there; the Windows build enabled LUAJIT_ENABLE_LUA52COMPAT. | LuaJIT is built with the Lua 5.2 extensions (and LUAJIT_DISABLE_BUFFER) on every platform, so those scripts load on Linux as on Windows. | User, 2026-10-05: "Enable on Linux", answering the L6 third-party corpus. Validation: LuaHelper.LuaJitBuildMatchesTheLegacyPlatform, automation_thirdparty_tests. |
| C03-ycbcr-on-load | Legacy SubsLoader::LoadASS added or replaced Script Info's "YCbCr Matrix" with TV.601 whenever it was missing or "None" (SubsLoader.cpp:167-168), so scripts saw it and every save wrote it. | An ASS file's Script Info stays as loaded; the matrix line changes only when the user edits it (Script properties). | User, 2026-10-05: "Keep the file as loaded", after the L6 third-party comparison (extends C03-preservation). Validation: ass load/save tests. |
| R1-pcre2 | Legacy wxRegEx (wxWidgets 3.3.3) runs PCRE2 10.47 (UTF, ALT_BSUX, DOTALL unless newline mode) after converting Tcl-style metasyntax (directors, embedded options, \\m \\M \\y \\Y, basic syntax). The rewrite had approximated it with std::wregex (ECMAScript) in Select lines, the editor's tag commands and the new search cards. | Every port of a legacy wxRegEx uses core::LegacyRegex: PCRE2 10.47 (16-bit, as wxString on Windows) with wxRegEx's own pattern conversions, flags, match offsets and Replace back-references (\\0-\\9, &). PCRE2 and Hunspell become vcpkg dependencies. | User, 2026-10-04: "migrate fully to pcre2", answering the F1/F4 reviews. Validation: `legacy_regex_tests` (conversions, flags, Replace) and the callers' tests. |
| R1-stale-table | Legacy compares A-B, then A-C, and leaves B's table on its grid; after Turn off an edit of B reaches SetModified, which runs SubsComparison with CG1/CG2 null and crashes (SubsGridBase.cpp:1136-1138, 1749). A table shorter than its grid (B edited after the A-C compare) throws at Comparison->at(key) (SubsGridWindow.cpp:419). | Only the compared pair has tables: Compare drops the earlier pair's, Turn off leaves none, and a row past a table paints plainly. | User, 2026-10-05, answering the wave-5 review. Validation: `subtitle_comparison_tests` TablesFollowCompareRemoveAndReplace, `comparison_tests` anEarlierPairKeepsNoTable, `line_model_tests` comparisonRolesFollowTheTable (rows past the table). |
| R1-session-off | Legacy Notebook::LoadLastSession destroys every tab without RemoveComparison (Notebook.cpp:1388-1392), so hasCompare stays on with CG1/CG2 dangling and Turn off reaches the destroyed grids (SubsGridBase.cpp:1889-1897). | Loading a session turns the comparison off. | User, 2026-10-05, answering the wave-5 review. Validation: `comparison_tests` loadingASessionTurnsComparisonOff. |
| R1-check-items-close | Legacy's check items (ITEM_CHECK) kept their menu open when switched: MenuDialog::SendEvent flips the check, repaints and queues the event without HideMenus (HikariSub/Menu.cpp:662-670), for the pointer and the keys (SendEvent from the key paths, Menu.cpp:1232, 1328, 1462, 1524), so several comparison criteria could be set before Compare in one opening (Notebook.cpp:932-936). With Shift a check item takes the plain item's path, HideMenus and the hotkey mapping (Menu.cpp:662, 672-682). ITEM_CHECK_AND_HIDE (Menu.cpp:626-629: Last session's two items, the text menu's Spellchecker, the font catalog and tag list options) and ITEM_RADIO items closed it. A Qt Quick Controls Menu closes on every item without a submenu (QQuickMenuPrivate::onItemTriggered). | Legacy kept, app-wide: a ShellMenuItem that is checkable keeps its menus open when switched by the pointer, Enter or Space, the status bar keeping its help; Shift+click closes them and maps as before. The items that were ITEM_CHECK_AND_HIDE or ITEM_RADIO in legacy, and the rewrite's own choices of one among several (layout presets, a visual tool's choices), set keepsMenuOpen false and close. | User, 2026-10-12. Validation: `comparison_tests` rightClickOnATabComparesIt (a style checked, the menus still open, Compare in the same opening); `shell_tests` checkItemsKeepTheirMenuOpen (File's "Save subtitles using the video name" by click, Return and Space with its help kept; the Grid's Hide columns and Filtering items), hotkeyGestureOnMenus (Shift+click on "Reverse filtering" maps and closes the menus). |
| R1-compare-reference | Legacy's Compare paired the active tab (CG1) with the tab the menu was opened on (CG2), both ordinary editable tabs (Notebook.cpp:915, 947-953); Ctrl+Q on a compared grid showed the partner (SubsGrid::OnShowPreview, SubsGrid.cpp:1416-1424). Legacy had no reference that is no tab. | Legacy pairing kept: Compare pairs two ordinary tabs and never makes CG2 the reference; the card's "Protected reference" is the partner Ctrl+Q shows in the reference tray (R2). Added: "Compare with reference" in the reference tray's header menu pairs the editing target (CG1) with the Protected reference (CG2), so a reference opened as no tab (openReference), which the tab menu cannot reach, can be compared; the tray paints its side, and the reference stays read-only and unwritten. Closing or replacing that reference closes no Document: it stays open as a tab (as any ended reference does), so the pair goes on as two tabs, as legacy's, until Turn off or either tab closes (Notebook::DeletePage, Notebook.cpp:241-242; legacy's DestroyPreview, SubsGridPreview.cpp:91-107, left the comparison). | User, 2026-10-12. Validation: `reference_tests` compareWithAReferenceThatIsNoTab (the header menu's item, both Grids' states, the reference's history, dirty flag and file unchanged), aComparedGridPreviewsItsPartner (Ctrl+Q shows the compared partner), closingANoTabReferenceKeepsItsComparisonAsATab (closed and replaced, the pair stays as tabs; closing the tab removes it); `comparison_tests` rightClickOnATabComparesIt; `shell_tests` panelMenuHoldsMoveUndockAndClose (the tray menu's items). |
| R2-hunspell | Legacy checks spelling with its vendored Hunspell. | The rewrite links Hunspell 1.7.3 from vcpkg behind its spelling port, reading .aff/.dic pairs from a Dictionary folder; packaging dictionaries remains a distribution item. | User, 2026-10-04: "Add Hunspell". Validation: F3's tests with a test dictionary. |
| R3-hang-crash-loss | Legacy search and rule replacement can hang (wxRegEx::Replace never advances past an empty match; "End of text" finds loop forever), crash (Fix minor errors' stale positions after an earlier rule changed the text: std::out_of_range; reads past result lists) or lose data (replacing in files drops Comment lines, blank lines and later Lines; "From the selected line" takes no Line). | These are fixed, each named in its card's coverage row: an empty match is replaced once and the search moves on by one character; finds stop at the end of the text; positions are recomputed after each replacement; file replacement keeps every line it does not change; out-of-range reads are bounds-checked. Ordinary quirks stay characterized. | User, 2026-10-04: "Fix as departures", for the hangs, crashes, undefined reads and data loss the F1, F3 and F4 reviews found. Validation: each card's tests name the departure. User, 2026-10-04: "Extend R3", answering the integration review: the scope also covers A1's audio cases (a failed cache block, cache create/write errors, a short cache read, "Not enough memory to cache the audio", the helper lost while open), the Linux Rules.txt read that loses the last rule's checkbox on every restart (F4-rules-cr) and the Linux Shift-JIS decode that turns every backslash into U+00A5 so a file replace rewrites every tag (F1-sjis-backslash). User, 2026-10-04, answering the integration verification: also O1-destroyed-results (DestroyDialogs closes Find and replace's and Fix minor errors' result windows with their tool; legacy left them over the deleted tool, a use-after-free) and A1-trim-in-use (the Linux audio cache trim keeps the cache in use, which legacy deleted). |
| R4-uchardet | Legacy OpenWrite::FileOpen detects a file's charset with uchardet (CheckCharSet) before decoding; the rewrite's file-scope readers had only UTF-8, UTF-16 with BOM and the system code page. | Files the legacy app read through CheckCharSet (find and replace in files, Rules.txt, other legacy text files) detect their charset with uchardet (vcpkg port) as legacy does; changed files are written back as legacy wrote them (UTF-8 with BOM). | User, 2026-10-04: "Add uchardet", answering the F1 round-2 review. Validation: the callers' charset fixtures (cp1250, cp1252, UTF-16). |
| R5-per-platform | Legacy behaviour differed by platform where the Windows and Linux builds differ (e.g. Windows text-mode reads fold CRLF to LF while the Linux build keeps "\\r"; wxDir listing order). | Reproduce each legacy platform's behaviour on that platform, quirks included, and pin both in tests where practical. | User, 2026-10-04: "Per platform". Validation: platform-conditional tests. |
| R6-dictionary-location | Legacy keeps UserDic.udic and the Hunspell pairs in <program folder>/Dictionary, which an installed program folder makes read-only. | UserDic.udic and user-added dictionaries live in <settings folder>/Dictionary; bundled dictionaries are also read from <program folder>/Dictionary. The settings importer carries legacy files over. | User, 2026-10-04: "Settings folder". Validation: F3's dictionary tests. |
| F1-utf16-bom | Legacy Windows decoded uchardet's "UTF-16" with the native little-endian converter: the BOM stayed as U+FEFF (a write-back added a second BOM) and a big-endian file was byte-swapped, so Find in files found nothing in it and Replace wrote nothing. The Linux build read no UTF-16 or UTF-32 file with a BOM at all: wxString's conversion ran iconv twice on one handle, the second pass read the BOM as U+FEFF and overflowed its counted buffer, so FileOpen read "" and the file was skipped (checked against wxWidgets 3.2.11 on glibc 2.44 during the integration review). | Both platforms remove the BOM and decode little- and big-endian UTF-16 (and UTF-32) correctly. | User, 2026-10-04: "Keep correct decoding", answering the integration review. Validation: legacy_text_file tests with LE and BE UTF-16 on both platforms. |
| F1-win-charsets | Legacy Windows read a file whose uchardet charset wx had no converter for (UHC Korean, ISO-8859-16, TIS-620, IBM852/855/865/866, MAC-*, GB18030, HZ, EUC-TW, ISO-2022-CN/KR, VISCII, GEORGIAN-*) as Latin-1, and Replace in files wrote that mojibake back as UTF-8, corrupting the file. | On Windows those charsets are decoded with ICU's converter for the name; a charset ICU cannot decode, or bytes it cannot decode, read as nothing and the file is skipped (as legacy skipped undecodable files). | User, 2026-10-04: "Fix as a departure", answering the integration verification. Validation: legacy_text_file tests for those names on Windows. |
| P6-session-crlf | Legacy Linux writes LastSession.txt / .kls with CRLF but reads it keeping each "\r", so a session it wrote restores as an empty first tab with every path ending in "\r" and unresolved, and each write-back adds another "\r". | Session files fold CRLF on Linux as on Windows, so Linux restores its own sessions. | User, 2026-10-04: "Fold CRLF on Linux", answering the P6 implementation (R3 data loss). Validation: session_file_tests on both platforms. |
| P6-session-review | Legacy Load last session (and loading a session file) destroys the open tabs without asking about unsaved changes. | Loading a session reviews unsaved work first, as Quit does (the P1 close review); Cancel keeps the open tabs. | User, 2026-10-04: "Review first", answering the P6 implementation (R3 data loss). Validation: session_tabs_tests. |
| WS1-session-layout | Legacy session files (LastSession.txt, .kls) have no panel layout: legacy had one window arrangement for every tab. | Each tab is written with a `Layout: ` line after its other fields: the tab's panel arrangement as one line of JSON (Hikari's layout envelope, ADR 0020). Legacy's reader skips lines it does not know, so legacy still loads these sessions with every other field; a session without the lines restores each tab as a new tab. | Owner decision 2026-10-18 on [WS1 #215](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/215) ("The Session saves and restores each tab's arrangement"). Validation: session_file_tests EachTabKeepsItsLayoutLine, workspace_layout_tests (Session round trip and recovery). |
| WS2-hikari-section | Legacy HikariSub and Aegisub keep no panel layout in a subtitle file (legacy had one window arrangement; Aegisub's own data goes in `[Aegisub Project Garbage]`). Legacy LoadASS (HikariSub/SubsLoader.cpp:94-170) takes any line outside `[Fonts]`/`[Graphics]` that has a `:` into Script Info, and any line starting `Dial`/`Comm` as a Line, whatever section it is in; it drops unknown headers. libass reads lines after an unknown header with the section before it, so lines after `[Fonts]` become font data (ass.c process_line, process_fonts_line). | An ASS or SSA file keeps its Document tab's panel arrangement in a `[Hikari]` section: `Layout=` and the arrangement's envelope (ADR 0020) in base64, one line. The section holds `key=value` lines, which have no `:` for legacy to take into Script Info; unknown keys, comments and malformed lines in it are kept byte for byte, and a key's last value wins. The arrangement keeps the docked panels' places and proportions (the main window's size, never its position), the preset name and the saved layout it started from (WS3; opening the file where no saved layout has that name gives the preset alone, as a Session from another profile does), and the floating panels' places from the main window's top-left; the screens and each window's screen are left out. A save or an autosave writes it: the value replaced in place, or a new section at the end of the file after a blank line, before the `[Fonts]`/`[Graphics]` data that may end it (with the sections libass reads as part of that data, up to a header it knows); a `[Hikari]` section inside that data is part of it to libass, so the value leaves it (the section too when only blank lines are left) and goes in a section outside it; an unchanged value changes no byte. A layout change alone marks nothing modified, and the Document takes the value once the write succeeds, as legacy's active Line (S4); an autosave writes it into its recovery file only, so a save with the setting off does not write it. Opening a file gives its tab the file's arrangement (also into the current tab, and for a recovered autosave or a legacy autosave; a Matroska track has none, as Demux keeps only its Script Info, Styles and Lines), its floating panels placed from the main window as it is then; a restored Session's own `Layout:` line takes precedence, and a Session tab without one takes the file's, also when its missing file is retried or relinked later. A file without the section, or whose value does not validate (named in the log, silently), follows WS1's new-tab rule; validation also refuses, before the docking engine sees them, what the engine would index or dereference unchecked or read outside its own catch (a floating or docked panel in a window Hikari does not have, a floating place in a window the file does not have, a tab index below -1 or a tab or place index no int holds, a window place no window can have (moved from the main window, it would leave the int), a layout tree value of another type, groups and layout items that do not match) and more than Hikari's panels can fill, and a value longer than any valid layout encodes to is refused before it is decoded. "Save panel layout in subtitle files" (`subtitles.layoutInFile`, Settings > Subtitle properties until PF1 places it, on by default) off: the section is neither read nor written, and an existing one stays as it is. SRT, TXT, MicroDVD and the other formats keep the arrangement only in the Session. The renderer script leaves out the section's header and properties; a Dialogue or Comment in it is a Line (LoadASS), drawn as the others; the renderer script writes every Line from outside `[Events]` under `[Events]`, as legacy GetVisible does, after its `Format:` line (before its first Line when it has none), with that section's own Lines before the `Format:` line after them, so every Line keeps the Document's order. | Owner decision 2026-10-18 on [WS2 #216](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/216) (a new `[Hikari]` section, not `[Aegisub Project Garbage]`). Validation: `hikari_core_hikari_section_tests` (read, in-place write, repeated sections, new section at the end and before trailing `[Fonts]`/`[Graphics]`, a value inside embedded data moved out of it, unknown keys, malformed lines, no `:` in what it writes, SSA, other formats, renderer script, Lines in the section drawn under `[Events]`); `AssSave.RendererScriptPutsEveryLineUnderEvents`, `RendererScriptPutsHeldLinesAfterTheEventsFormat`, `RendererScriptKeepsTheOrderOfLinesBeforeTheEventsFormat`; `document_files_tests` SaveWritesTheLayoutIntoTheHikariSection, OnlyAssSavesWriteTheLayout; `hikari_ui_file_layouts_tests` (file form without screen positions; save and reopen in a new start with the main window elsewhere; a new tab, the current tab, the Session's precedence, a retried or relinked Session file, the setting off, unusable values, a layout larger than Hikari's panels, a docked place in a missing window, a layout tree the engine cannot read, a panel position the engine cannot read, a recovered autosave and a legacy autosave, SRT, the saved layout the arrangement started from). Compatibility evidence: see the [WS2 coverage row](coverage.md). |

| Y9-mkv-every-block | FFMS_GetSubtitles (Thirdparty/Build/FFMS2/indexing_additional.cpp:81-105 at 20d647c4, the file the hikari-ffms2 port builds) rewound the indexer's demuxer with `av_seek_frame(Track, 0, 0)` before reading the track. That goes to the track's first index entry, and libavformat keeps one entry per timestamp, at the position the last cue of that time names. "Kage 07 - Opening TH Softsub (1080p AAC Fonts).mkv" keeps 38 blocks at 0:00:00.401 (every strip of its first Thai line, "เดิมพันด้วยทุกสิ่งที่มีในตอนนี้", each a 38.7 KB \p1 drawing) in its first cluster and 401 more of that time in the second, and the last of those cues names the second cluster, so 12258 of the track's 12296 blocks were read and the line was never in the Document: no renderer showed it. Legacy's own load lost it the same way. | The media helper reads the track with a demuxer of its own, opened as the indexer's was, from the start of the file without a seek (only that track demuxed): every block arrives, in file order. Times, the total, the blocks under 2 bytes left out and the text up to its first NUL are the extension's. Legacy's FFMS_GetSubtitles reads the same way (branch lc-thai). | User, 2026-10-14 (decision 28, "fix ... when play on hikari it did not show the first thai line ... on every sub renderers"): user decision. Validation: `backends_matroska_tests` BlocksBeforeTheCuedClusterArriveAndTheirLineDraws (hikari_mkv_fixture "scanline": six 38.7 KB scanline strips in one cluster, two blocks in the next, the last cue at their time naming the next; all eight blocks whole and in order, and the first line staged as legacy built it and rendered by libass at 0:00:02 drawing under its \pos and nowhere else; 2 of 8 blocks with the old helper); `backends_matroska_capture_tests` still equal to legacy's probe on the mkvmerge files; the real file's frame 52 (0:00:02.169) shows the line (it did not before). |

Validation: shell_tests openingAnMkvFromOpenSubtitlesLoadsItsTrack ("SHOUT.MKV" offers its tracks and its SubRip track loads into a new tab as "SHOUT.srt"). |
| Y9-subtitle-timebase | FFMS_GetSubtitles (Thirdparty/Build/FFMS2/indexing_additional.cpp:119-121 after legacy ccc8ad3f7; the helper's readSubtitles copied it) handed Demux::GetSubtitles `Packet->pts / Packet->time_base.den` and `Packet->duration / Packet->time_base.den` as milliseconds. av_read_frame leaves AVPacket.time_base at {0, 1}, so the times stayed in the stream's own ticks: right only when the stream's time base is 1/1000, which is Matroska with the default TimestampScale. A Matroska file at 100 us ticks loaded ten times too late, one at 10 ms ticks ten times too early, MP4/MOV mov_text (1/600, 1/90000, 1/1000000 as ffmpeg writes it) and MPEG-TS (1/90000) hundreds of times off. The progress total divided by `time_base.den / 1000.f`. | The media helper rescales each packet with av_rescale_q from the stream's time base to milliseconds: start and end (pts + duration) each to the nearest millisecond, and the duration taken between them, so the end is the nearest millisecond to the packet's end. Times stay on the container's own clock (no start_time is taken off), as FFMS2 gives the video's frame times. A packet without a pts uses its dts, and one with neither starts at 0 (where the old value, AV_NOPTS_VALUE cut to an int, also landed); a negative duration is 0. The total is rescaled the same way, AV_NOPTS_VALUE passed through. Matroska at the default TimestampScale is unchanged to the millisecond. Legacy's extension gets the same fix (branch lc-timebase), and its FFMS_GetChapters, which divided by `time_base.den / 1000` and so by zero for QuickTime's 1/600 chapter track, now rescales too (the helper's chapters already used av_rescale_q). | User, 2026-10-14 (decision 38d, "subtitle time base: legacy pts/time_base.den only right for 1 ms containers — fix, check Qt"): user decision. Validation: `backends_matroska_tests` SubtitleTimesAreMillisecondsInEveryTimeBase (Matroska at TimestampScale 100000 and 10000000 from hikari_mkv_fixture "scale100us" and "scale10ms", mov_text in MP4 at 1/600 and in MOV at 1/90000 and DVB teletext in MPEG-TS at 1/90000 from hikari_media_fixture "subs-mp4", "subs-mov" and "subs-ts": exact start, duration and progress in milliseconds per packet) and SubtitleTimesAtATenthOfAMillisecondMakeTheirLines (the lines staged as legacy built them); the existing Matroska cases and `backends_matroska_capture_tests` keep their times. |
| N3-script-attachments | Legacy never handed a script's attachments to a renderer: SubsGrid::GetVisible (HikariSub/SubsGridBase.cpp:1797-1830 at a8e4a9b4b) built the text for csri_open_mem and ass_read_memory from GetAssHeader without SubsFile's embedded sections, which only SaveFile wrote (SubsGridBase.cpp:427), so VSFilterMod drew `\1img(<attached image>)` as a white fill and no renderer saw a font from [Fonts]. | S4-validation-edits | Legacy LuaCommand::Validate (HikariSub/Automation.cpp:936-969) hands the validation function an AutoToFile over the open file (Automation.cpp:944), which edits it in place (HikariSub/AutomationToFile.cpp:561 ObjectIndexWrite, :693 ObjectDelete, :804 ObjectAppend, :840 ObjectInsert). When validation then answers false (or raises an error, :952-956) the macro does not run, but the edits stay in the file without an undo step. | When a script's validation function edits the subtitles and then answers false (or raises an error), the edits are dropped and the Document stays unchanged: content, Script Info, Styles, dirty state and history. Edits a validation function makes before answering true still reach the macro and apply with it as its one step (as legacy). | User, 2026-10-05: approved with the wave-5 batch-2 review. Validation: `macro_tests` AResultThatFailedValidationChangesNothing (applyMacroResult refuses a result whose validation failed as Invalid); `lua_helper_tests` ValidationEditsAreDroppedWhenItAnswersFalse ("Edits then false" and "Edits then error" stage nothing; the Document, dirty state and history unchanged), ValidationDecidesWhetherTheMacroRuns (a false answer stages nothing); `automation_document_scripts_tests` validationEditsAreDroppedWhenItAnswersFalse ("Run the last loaded script" and the menu, real helper). |
| S4-run-validation | Legacy Automation::BuildMenu (HikariSub/Automation.cpp:1340) validates every autoload and Document-script macro with a validation function each time the Automation menu is built and disables the items that answer false (:1375, :1434). A hotkey goes through its menu item, and OnMenuSelected rebuilds that menu first (HikariSubFrame.cpp:680-686), so "Run the last loaded script" (and a script hotkey) validated every Document-script macro before LuaCommand::Validate and Run of the one it runs (legacy capture local-s4-document-scripts-20261013); each of those validations could edit the file in place. | Opening the Automation menu validates no macro, and its items stay enabled. Running a macro (from the menu, a script hotkey or "Run the last loaded script") validates the macros of the scripts it depends on and then its own, and no other. A script depends on another loaded script (autoload or Document script, loaded and ready) when its top level loaded that script's file as Lua code: by `require` (DependencyControl's requireModules calls require) or `include`, directly or through a file it loaded (`dofile` and `loadfile` are nil in the helper, as legacy sets them (HikariSub/Automation.cpp:555-559); code a script reads itself and passes to `load` or `loadstring` is not a file it loaded). The automation API has no call from one macro into another, and required modules have no validation functions, so a script's code running in another script's macros is the only dependency a validation can belong to. Each dependency's macros with a validation function are validated in registration order, the scripts in the order their files were first loaded, each once, in their own helpers on the run's snapshot; what they stage is dropped. A dependency that is unloaded or reloading by the time its turn comes is skipped, as one that was not ready when the run began. The first that answers false or raises an error stops the run the way the macro's own validation does: the error goes to the log window, a hotkey or "Run the last loaded script" shows "Validation Lua script '<file>' failed" naming that dependency, the menu says nothing, and the Document is unchanged. | User decision, 2026-10-13: "validate only macro that runs but if the macro have dependencies validate that too". Validation: `macro_validation_tests` (only the ready scripts whose files the script loaded, their validated macros in order, each once, never itself); `lua_helper_tests` LoadListsTheFilesTheTopLevelLoaded (require, nested require and include are listed, a macro's later require is not), ValidateCallsOnlyTheValidationFunction; `automation_document_scripts_tests` runValidatesItsDependenciesFirst (an autoload dependency's two validated macros and a Document-script dependency's, then the macro's own, then the run; an unrelated script's and the script's other macro's validations do not run; the first or a later dependency answering false or raising, and the macro's own answering false after its dependencies passed, block the run with the same box and log, naming the script that failed, and nothing from the menu; a dependency unloaded while an earlier one validates is skipped), replaysTheLegacyCapture (legacy's menu-build validations are left out of its records). User, 2026-10-13 night (decision 15): a dependency is a whole loaded script, every one of its macros with a validation function must pass, and a failing dependency's box names its script (dep.lua); the load-time-only rule and the skip of a script that is not ready are accepted as built. |

| T2-move-untimed | Visuals::GetPosnScale (HikariSub/Visuals.cpp:648-675) reads only the values a `\move` has into the caller's table and then adds the Line's start to its times (`tbl[4] += Start; tbl[5] += Start`); a `\move` with six values replaces them first, one with four does not. Move::SetCurVisual passes its own `moveValues` member (HikariSub/VisualMove.cpp:355-371), so for `\move(x1,y1,x2,y2)` Move's start and end times are the Line's start on the first read, twice it on the second and so on; for `\move(...,0,0)` (any two times both 0 or less) both are the Line's start (or before it). Move's cross (DrawVisual, VisualMove.cpp:36-93) therefore jumps to the end at the Line's start, or holds the start until a later multiple, the two-point move extrapolates over no time, and a drag after a second read wrote the doubled start back as the `\move`'s times (`...,1000,1000)` on a Line from 1 s). libass moves such a `\move` over the whole Line (libass/ass_parse.c, the `\move` tag: `if (t1 <= 0 && t2 <= 0) { t1 = 0; t2 = Duration; }`, times as whole numbers, swapped when t1 > t2). Found by the T2 libass comparison (`VisualMoveRender`). | Move follows libass: `visual::posnScale` (src/application/visual_script.cpp) reads 0 for times a `\move` lacks instead of the previous read's, and `visual::moveInterval` gives MoveTool::setCurVisual (src/application/visual_position.cpp) the interval libass moves over: a `\move` with four values, or with six whose times are both 0 or less, runs from the Line's start to its end; otherwise its own times, the earlier first. The cross, the start's square until the move begins, the end's circle once it is over and the two-point extrapolation (setMove) read that interval, so the cross sits on the text libass draws over the whole Line. The written `\move` keeps the text's own times (an untimed one is written `0,0`, as legacy's first read wrote it), which libass reads as the whole Line, and the T1/T2 values show them. Not changed here (the same assumption for `0,0`, outside this decision): the Position tool's `\move` table (`visual::linePosition`: no times is already the whole Line, `0,0` is the Line's start), T3/T6's calcMovePos and T4's drawing::movePosition (no times is the whole Line, `0,0` the start), the Grid's split at the video time (`0,0` is the whole Line, two negative times are not), the batch path of Move (a target without six values takes the active Line's GetMoveTimes). | User, 2026-10-13, as a legacy-defect fix ("follow libass"). Validation: `visual_move_render_tests` VisualMoveRender.AMoveWithoutTimesOrWithZeroTimesFollowsLibassOverTheWholeLine (an `\an5` square under `\move(x1,y1,x2,y2)` and `\move(x1,y1,x2,y2,0,0)` on a 1-3 s Line, read twice, rendered by LibassRenderer at eight times from the Line's first to its last millisecond at three video / script ratios: Move's cross on the painted square's centroid at every time and its start square at the start, within 0.25 frame pixel); `visual_position_tests` VisualMoveTimes.AMoveWithoutTimesOrWithBothAtMostZeroRunsOverTheWholeLine (the interval for no times, `0,0`, `-200,0`, `-300,-100`, `0.5,0.9`, own times swapped and negative, `\pos` and no tag through GetMoveTimes; the table's times not added again on a second read) and VisualMoveTimes.MoveKeepsAnUntimedMoveUntimedOverRepeatedReads (moveStart/moveEnd 1000/3000 after two reads, T1/T2 0, two drags writing `0,0`, the cross halfway along at 2 s); VisualPositionCapture.ReplaysTheLegacyProbe unchanged (no Move case reads a `\move` without times or with `0,0` as the active Line). |

| V2-seek-bar-drag-throttle | Legacy VideoSlider::OnMouseEvent (HikariSub/VideoSlider.cpp:143-206, at 20d647c4; the fullscreen panel uses the same class, VideoFullscreen.cpp:51) sought on every mouse move while the thumb was held (`if (!VB->IsDirectShow()){ SendTime(msTimePosition); }`, 188) for its FFMS2 and GStreamer renderers, and for DirectShow only once, on the release (`SendTime` at 158-162); every SendTime was VideoBox::Seek(ms, true, SEEK_NO_SNAP). The Qt rewrite sought on every move of the docked and the fullscreen seek bar for every player (605c33aa2), a decode or a player restart per move. | Held and dragged, both seek bars seek at most once every 50 ms with every player (application::SeekThrottle through VideoController::seekBarMoved): the first move at once, then the newest frame each time the interval is up, never a frame already sought; the release seeks the frame it lands on unless that frame was sought within the last 50 ms (so a click seeks once). Paused, each seek shows its frame; playing, playback goes on from it, as before. Neither legacy behaviour is kept: DirectShow's seek-on-release showed nothing while dragging, the other players' seek-per-move outran the decoder. | User, 2026-10-14 (user decision, item 30). Validation: `seek_throttle_tests` (3: a one-second drag of 500 moves gives 20-22 seeks, increasing, the last one the release's frame; a waiting frame sought when its interval is up and replaced by newer moves; the release dropping a waiting frame and seeking its own, not repeating a frame just sought, repeating one held longer than the interval); `hikari_ui_playing_frame_tests` seekBarDragSeeksAtMostEvery50Ms and fullscreenSeekBarDragSeeksAtMostEvery50Ms (real pointer drags of 60 moves 4 ms apart over each bar: at least 30 moves reported, at least two seeks during the drag, at most one per 50 ms of the drag plus three and fewer than a third of the moves, the last seek and the session's frame equal to the bar's value where it was let go, nothing sought afterwards; paused the frame let go on is then shown, playing the fake player's last seek is to that frame's start and playback goes on). |

Legacy main makes the same change (HikariSub/NaturalOrder.cpp, item 34). | User, 2026-10-14 (user decision, item 34): natural name order in both apps instead of disk order. Validation run: `video_fullscreen_tests` VideoFullscreenMonitor.AnIndexTakesThatMonitor and OneMenuEntryPerMonitorAfterTheFirst (the choice by index over the list); Spix `hikari_ui_video_fullscreen_workflow.twoscreens` (Linux offscreen, `--monitors 2`: the second screen chosen by the context menu's "Open in full screen on monitor 2") and `.secondprimary` (Linux offscreen, two screens with the second made the primary before the window opens: Qt's list, which `VideoFullscreenController::monitors` returns unchanged, is then the new primary first and the other screen after it, and "Open in full screen on monitor 2" takes the screen the system listed first, at its geometry). Run before this branch, not rerun on it: native gate step `videofs` (local evidence out/v5/native-gate-r2: monitor 2 on another output under X11 with two RandR monitors, sway, KWin and mutter, recorded in coverage.md's V5 row). Run before this branch, not rerun on it: native gate step `videofs` (out/v5/native-gate-r2) on sway (default focus_on_window_activation and `focus`), KWin and mutter, observing the keyboard in the fullscreen window on another output, which is a compositor's and not observable offscreen. | E4-wheel-until-send | The wheel over a time field or NumCtrl (Layer, margins) without the focus sets the field's text with SetValue(..., true), marking it modified, and posts NUMBER_CHANGED (TimeCtrl.cpp:363-385, NumCtrl.cpp:305-311). Showing another Line, Esc's discard, the Time/Frames switch and a time from the audio display drop it, and nothing is written back later. OnEdit still runs for a time field with the focus: with Start or End focused the draft takes a stepped Start or End and Duration follows, and with Duration focused End shows the draft's End again. A stepped field shows the warning colour until then (E4-stepped-field-warning). The audio display's commit (AUDIO_CHANGE_TIME) sends only the times it set; a stepped Layer or margin waits for the editor's next Send. | User, 2026-10-13 (user decision, evening item 3): "build the 'modified but not applied' field state so wheel stepping works as in legacy (text changes, field marked modified, applied on next Send)". The Line is not committed, the editor does not advance and the tag list does not put its selection; a second, separate Enter commits as usual (RoleField.compositionKey, src/ui/Main.qml). An Enter the Japanese IME kept (its release reaches the field) leaves the next Enter alone, however quick. Prior art: CodeMirror 6 and ProseMirror ignore one Enter keydown within 100 ms or 500 ms of compositionend. | User, 2026-10-16 (decision 60: "Hold the Enter"). Validation: `shell_tests` anEnterThatEndsACompositionOnlyEndsIt (the Windows order: preedit, commit, Return; the ibus-hangul order: preedit cleared, commit, Return; an Enter during the composition; the Japanese order: commit, the Enter's release, an immediate Enter that commits; a plain Enter) and tagListCompletesTagsInTheRawText (a separate Enter after a commit still puts the tag). Not yet observed with a real IME: the Microsoft Korean IME on Windows and ibus-hangul/fcitx5-hangul on Linux. |

A second, separate Escape reverts the draft as usual (K10's plain-draft case unchanged). Beyond the decision's list-open case, the same holds without the list: Escape during or with the end of a composition no longer reverts the draft (K10's first case changes), consistent with J5 and with edit-transactions.md ("Native composition is finished or cancelled first, never silently dropped"). | User, 2026-10-16 (decision 60: "Esc closes the list first"; the no-list case is recorded here as a further change in the same direction). Not yet observed with a real IME: whether the Microsoft Korean IME keeps or drops the syllable on Escape after the change (Qt's reset commits it, so the draft is expected to keep 한), and ibus-hangul/fcitx5-hangul on Linux. |

| SY2-colour-popover | Legacy had two colour pickers. "Choose color" (DialogColorPicker, HikariSub/ColorPicker.cpp) opened on a left click, centred or at the pointer, with a type choice across the four colours. The simple "Color picker" (SimpleColorPickerDialog, ColorPicker.cpp) opened on a right click at the pointer, captured the pointer at once (a held right button picked continuously outside it), could move itself to the picked spot ("Move the window to the color selection location"), had its own type choice (COLOR_TYPE_CHANGED) and added recent colours through the option text (Y7-recent-option-text). (1) A right click opens the popover with the eyedropper started (on Wayland the portal asked); `colourPicker.switchClicks` stays under More… and swaps the two clicks. (2) The simple window's extras retire: moving the window to the picked spot, the held-button capture outside it and opening at the pointer; the eyedropper keeps "Choose color"'s dropper protocol. (3) The colour type choice is dropped: each button and swatch opens the popover for its own colour. (4) Recent colours belong to the window that opened the popover, as DialogColorPicker::Get gave them; the option-text path stays in the controller with no caller. (5) The Style editor opens the popover at the swatch's colour and its transparency field, either click. (6) Settings → Appearance, the Automation dialog's colour controls and the Dummy video swatch use the popover, without Alpha where the value has none; Esc restores the value before. Changes apply live; Esc takes them back (Cancel); Enter, a press outside or a press on the anchor keeps them and adds the colour to the recent ones (OK). Narrower than proposed: the colour before is shown but not clickable, as a pick of it would leave the earlier tag in the Line text (the Line editor edits the current text, as legacy did); Esc is the way back. | User, 2026-10-19: "approved as written, all six items" ([#245](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/245#issuecomment-6264684335), answering the proposal at [#245](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/245#issuecomment-6259601012)). Its CSRI name stays `vsfiltermod_textsub`, so stored choices, the label and the list order (W2-libass-sole-default) are unchanged; it is still never the default. Unlike r5.2.6 (which refused it) r5.3.1 and the fork take CSRI_F_BGRA, but draw it premultiplied with inverted alpha (255 clear) and only multiply the frame's alpha by the coverage, so on the adapter's cleared overlay the alpha stayed 0 and nothing showed (seen in the Winix VM on the CI package, 2026-10-14); the adapter therefore asks VSFilterMod for BGR_ alone, the target legacy's r5.2.6 was always drawn on, and keeps BGRA then BGR_ for any other CSRI renderer (Csri.VsfilterModIsAskedForBgrxEvenThoughItTakesBgra). 2026-10-14 (user decisions 29 and 38b): re-pinned to r5.3.1-hikari.2, which keeps BGR_ byte-identical to hikari.1 and draws BGRA with straight alpha (255 opaque) over the frame's (fork e51456f "Draw CSRI_F_BGRA frames with straight alpha"); hikari.1 (archive c9e6326b..., DLL 68a16573..., run 38528631150 on d1737a93) before it. With hikari.2 the adapter asks VSFilterMod for BGRA again, then BGR_, as any CSRI renderer (Csri.VsfilterModIsAskedForBgraThenBgrx): one drawing per frame where the BGR_ route drew each instant onto black and onto white. In the Winix VM (job-b4271fbf, 2026-10-15) VsfilterMod.OverlayMatchesDrawingOnTheFrame gives the same covered pixels and the same largest difference (2 levels) against VSFilterMod's own BGR_ drawing onto the frame as the BGR_ route did (job-78b20daa) on all 14 frames, and VsfilterMod.DrawsTheScriptsAttachedImageAndFonts draws the `\1img(red.png)` square red. | User decision, 2026-10-14 ("move the vsfiltermod to be a fork of https://github.com/Masaiki/VSFilterMod that fix every open issues"; decisions record item 27: a published release on altqx/VSFilterMod that both apps pin by URL and SHA-256). Validation: `cmake -DDOWNLOADS=<dir> -P cmake/HikariVsfilterMod.cmake` (archive and DLL hashes match); the Windows package workflow's payload step (Csri/VSFiltermod.dll present); on Windows the windows-x64-vsfiltermod build's `hikari_backends_vsfiltermod_tests` and, in the Winix VM, the package started on a fresh profile (libass, then VSFilterMod labelled) and VSFilterMod drawing the Kaguya-hime opening's first Thai line (Y9-mkv-every-block). |
| D3-engine-move | Legacy has no dockable or floating panels. Its only borderless windows are its own top-level frames and dialogs on Windows: the frame extends DWM's frame over its client area (HikariSub/HikariFrame.cpp:60-83, under `__WXMSW__`) and answers WM_NCHITTEST with HTCAPTION over its drawn title (HikariFrame.cpp:391-393; dialogs HikariDialog.cpp:667-668), so Windows itself moves them, with its snapping. On Linux they are ordinary decorated wxGTK windows (HikariFrame.cpp:84-88). Card #207 asked for floating panels "moved and resized through the compositor (startSystemMove/startSystemResize)" and for drag-to-dock with the drop highlight. | A floating panel's or floating window's header splits on Wayland and X11 (Docking::platformUsesSystemMove; src/ui/docking.cpp): its title or tabs are the engine's drag, which moves the window, shows the drop highlight and docks (on Wayland a drag-and-drop), and the rest of the header (right of the title, up to the "⋯" button; right of the tabs, up to the toolbar) moves the window through QWindow::startSystemMove once the pointer has moved (DockSystemMoveArea.qml), so the window system's snapping and tiling apply there and that part does not dock; a double-click there docks. The title ends where the free part starts, elided if it must be; short of room the title and the free part keep 48 pixels each. Where the platform declines the system move (an X server without a window manager that moves windows) the window follows the pointer itself. Resize stays startSystemResize from the drawn shadow everywhere. Until 2026-10-12 the split was Wayland's only and X11 and Windows moved by the engine's drag over the whole header. Since 2026-10-15 Windows has the engine's drag over the whole header again: the title, the part right of it and right of the tabs all drag with the drop highlight and dock, a double-click there docks and a right click opens the panel's menu; Windows' Snap never takes the borderless tool window during the free part's move (the Winix VM and real hardware, 2026-10-15), so the split offered nothing there, and the owner's first drag to dock on real hardware started right of the title, moved the window and did not dock. | User, 2026-10-12 ("split header everywhere"), recorded on [D3 #207](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/207); owner, 2026-10-15, for Windows ("On Windows, make the whole header the docking drag"). Validation: `docking_qualification_tests` headerSplitsOnWaylandAndX11, wholeFloatingHeaderDocksWhereItDoesNotSplit (the Windows header, offscreen), floatingHeaderDocksByItsTitleAndMovesByTheRest, floatingTabBarMovesRightOfItsTabs and floatingTitleKeepsItsRoom (offscreen, and the three at 125, 150, 175 and 200 % in the .scale runs), x11HeaderMovesThroughTheWindowSystem (the .xvfb run, where xvfb-run is installed); the native gate's move, pointer and drop-highlight steps on X11 and on Windows (Winix VM) are still owed. |
| D3-windows-snap | Legacy has no floating panels (D3-engine-move). Its own borderless frames and dialogs on Windows kept Windows' Snap: Windows moved them itself, through WM_NCHITTEST answering HTCAPTION over the drawn title on a frame that extends DWM's frame (HikariSub/HikariFrame.cpp:60-83, 391-393; HikariDialog.cpp:667-668). | On Windows, Snap is not offered for a floating panel or floating window, by design: since 2026-10-15 its whole header is the engine's drag (D3-engine-move; `Docking::platformUsesSystemMove` is Wayland and X11 only), so no part of it starts the window system's move (QWindow::startSystemMove) that Snap acts on. Dragged by its header to a monitor's left or top edge and held there, it follows the pointer, shows no Snap layout and is not resized; it stays floating where it is dropped, partly off the screen if dropped there, and a drop over the main window docks it with the drop highlight. The floating windows are borderless tool windows (`Qt::Tool \| Qt::FramelessWindowHint`, KDDockWidgets' FloatingWindow::s_windowFlagsOverride in src/ui/docking.cpp). Until 2026-10-15 the header's free part was the window system's move, and Snap did not take the tool window during it either; the main window keeps Snap. | User, 2026-10-15 (decision 50: "Windows Snap not taking the borderless floating panel during a free-part move is accepted"), then the owner's choice of 2026-10-15 to make the whole header the docking drag on Windows (D3-engine-move, 7812c180f), which removes the free-part move there, so Snap is no longer offered. Validation before that change: Windows native gate `floating-snap` at 9aa897065 (gate_windows.py in the Winix VM; evidence-H/windows/gate/snap.json, snap-left-held.png, snap-top-held.png): the floating Audio window's free part dragged to the primary monitor's left edge and to its top edge, held 1.5 s and released: not snapped either time (left: 649,59 639x178 to -373,345 639x178; top: to -77,0 639x178, the size unchanged), still a floating panel after each; the same on real hardware (branch evidence/win-hw-20261015, d3 item 6). Since the change the gate's `snap` step expects the engine's drag from the header right of the title: the window follows the pointer while held, is not snapped, keeps its size and stays floating (`floating-snap`), and the `pointer` step's `floating-free-part-docks` docks it from there; `docking_qualification_tests` wholeFloatingHeaderDocksWhereItDoesNotSplit and headerSplitsOnWaylandAndX11. On Linux the window manager's edge tiling does take a free-part move where it offers that (KWin and mutter in the native gate; openbox has none). |
| D3-main-window-minimum | Legacy's main frame has a fixed minimum on Windows only: WM_GETMINMAXINFO answers 600 x 400 whatever its panels need (HikariSub/HikariFrame.cpp:370-374). Validation: shell_tests `mainWindowKeepsTheDockedLayoutsMinimum`. |

| T3-scale-shift-hover | Scale::OnMouseEvent runs its press code (HikariSub/VisualScale.cpp:298-325) on a click and also whenever Shift's state differs from the one it last recorded (`wasUsedShift != evt.ShiftDown()`), with or without a button down. So while only hovering, pressing or releasing Shift put up a drag's shape: SIZEWE (or the axis the last press left) away from the arrows' ends until the next move there, and SIZING over the width's end with "Preserve proportions" until the pointer left the ends. A release with Shift still held ran it again right after the release's arrow (279-284), so the drag's shape stayed up after the drag. | Shift changes Scale's pointer shape only while a button is down: on a press and during a drag, where pressing Shift still shows SIZING. While hovering, the shape follows the hover rules alone (the ends' shapes, VisualScale.cpp:286-297, the arrow elsewhere), and a release with Shift held leaves the hover shape at once. ScaleTool::pointer (src/application/visual_scale.cpp) still sets the press code's state (the type, the offsets, the last scale, the recorded Shift) as legacy did; only the shape is held back. | User, 2026-10-13 (drop the quirk; Shift matters only during a drag). Validation: `visual_transform_tests` VisualTransformPointer.ScaleShiftChangesTheShapeOnlyDuringADrag (Shift pressed and released while hovering away from the ends and over the width's end, a Shift drag released with Shift still held away from the ends and over the width's end, and Shift over the width's end with "Preserve proportions", hovering and on a Shift drag's release there; legacy's code fails seven of its checks, showing SIZEWE away from the ends while hovering, SIZING after the Shift drag's release there and SIZING over the width's end with "Preserve proportions"; without that option legacy's press code gives the width's end its own SIZEWE, so those steps pass on both); `shell_tests` visualScaleAndRotationsThroughThePanel (the overlay's cursorShape: the arrow on a Shift move while hovering and after it, SizeAll on a Shift press, the arrow right after the release with Shift held, and no step); VisualCapture.ReplaysTheLegacyT3Probe unchanged (the replay compares no pointer shape). |
