| Y4-playres-ask | SubsGrid::GetASSRes (HikariSub/SubsGridBase.cpp:1653-1679 at 20d647c4) writes a missing PlayResX/PlayResY into the file whenever anything reads the resolution (1280x720 when both are missing, else the 16:9 partner of the one present; AddSInfo with no undo step, its SetModified commented out): the status bar, the visual tools, the video box, resampling and LuaGetScriptResolution (AutomationToFile.cpp:1083-1095), so a macro calling subtitles.script_resolution() (karaskel's collect_head among them, karaskel-auto4.lua:106) saw the keys written silently and they reached the file with its next save. The S4 capture shows them on every validation and run. | Reading the resolution never writes (scriptResolution and the status bar, visual tools and resampling keep legacy's values read only). When a macro reads it (subtitles.script_resolution, from its processing or validation function) and the Document lacks PlayResX or PlayResY, the run waits on a question ("Script resolution", ScriptResolutionDialog.qml): width and height start from the open video's own size, else legacy's values (1280x720, or the 16:9 partner); with a video and one key written, both keys still start from the video's size (PlayResY 480 with a 320x240 video: 320x240); "Use video resolution" puts the video's size back; Return presses the focused button, else Set; Set writes both keys as one "Setting the script resolution" undo step (applied before the macro's own step, whatever the macro's outcome) and the macro goes on with them, as legacy's did; Cancel, Escape or closing the window stops the macro with nothing changed and no message. "Always set it from the video without asking" keeps automation.scriptResolutionFromVideo (also Settings > Editor) and locks the fields on what it sets (unticked, the typed values come back); later runs then set both keys to the video's size without asking, replacing a key the file has; without a video they ask. "Use video resolution" and the check box show only with a video open. The step is kept when the macro then fails, is cancelled, its validation refuses or the Document changed under the run (the macro's result is then refused as stale, as before); a run cancelled after Set says "The macro was cancelled; only the script resolution was set.". A macro that never reads the resolution, or a Document that has both keys, is never asked about. Without a window to ask in (a composition with no view, the helper tests) nothing waits and nothing is written: the macro gets legacy's values (the rewrite's helper had returned the raw values, 0 for a missing key; it now returns legacy's). A Document that is not ASS is never asked about and gets legacy's values. | User, 2026-10-13 (evening decisions, Y4/S4): "should ask user to set it when about to use the automations that need it (with option to automatically set it also it should based on the current video res)". Export filters are not involved (register_filter stays a stub). Validation: `resample_tests` ScriptResolution.* (what is missing, legacy's values without a write, the suggestion: the video's size for both keys, one undoable step, staged Script Info as Document::setScriptInfo); `automation_services_tests` RouterTest.TheScriptResolutionQuestionGoesToItsPort; `lua_helper_tests` ScriptResolutionAsksTheHostWhileItIsMissing (asked only while missing, karaskel's collect_head too, the answer written into the staged Script Info, no answer: legacy's values, nothing written); `automation_resolution_tests` (composition, real helpers and media: the question with the video's size, legacy's values without a video, Cancel stops the macro, the resolution and the macro's edits as two steps, "Always" skips the question and replaces a written key with the video's size, a written key replaced in the suggestion, a validation read asked once, Cancel in validation, a refusing validation keeps the step, a Document changed under the run keeps the step and refuses the macro, nothing asked when nothing is missing or read, no window: nothing waits, a cancelled run withdraws the question, a run cancelled after Set keeps the step and says so, a dependency's validation that reads it asks once for the run (S4-run-validation: the resolution set there is staged for the later helpers)); `shell_tests` scriptResolutionQuestionSetsItBeforeTheMacroGoesOn (the dialog's fields, buttons, check box and its untick, Return in a field and on Cancel, Escape). User, 2026-10-13 night (decision 14): with only one key written, "Always set it from the video without asking" and the question's starting values replace both keys with the video's size; the set resolution stays its own undoable step even when the macro then fails, is cancelled or its validation refuses. |
| LF-libass-cmap | Legacy Windows builds libass 0.17.5 (`Thirdparty/libass` at `4a05d81`, "Release 0.17.5"); legacy Linux links the distribution's libass; the rewrite built 0.17.5 as well. 0.17.5 draws nothing for text in a font whose (3,0) symbol cmap does not start at U+F020 (at U+F120 or U+0020), for U+F0xx text in such a font, or in a legacy simplified Arabic font (charset 178) that has a Unicode cmap. | The rewrite renders with the altqx/libass fork (Q1, `ports/libass` at `4f5ea436`), which has upstream libass's changes after 0.17.5 `057a533` (the symbol base offset comes from the first cmap code, so cmaps at U+F120 and U+0020 work and U+F0xx text maps onto them) and `b085eeb` (legacy Arabic charsets on Unicode cmaps), so that text draws. Nothing else renders differently from 0.17.5: the fork reads text by grapheme cluster, but after its Prepend fix (`4f5ea43`, a cluster stops before `{` and `\`) it renders the fork's `grapheme.ass` the same as 0.17.5 at every frame (500 frames at 20 ms over 0-10 s, the Prepend cases at 8-10 s included) and 9,000 random events (Prepend characters with override blocks, every escape, tabs, drawings, combining marks, ZWJ, regional indicators, jamo, 30-45 codepoint clusters; WrapStyle 0 and 2) the same as 0.17.5; the 7 original golden cases are identical. So there is no grapheme departure. | User, 2026-10-15: keep the fork's behaviour as the default, with no flag (decision 2 of the libass fork plan; brain `decisions/hikari-libass-fork-open-decisions-2026-10-15`). Validation: `libass_renderer_tests` LibassFork.SymbolAndLegacyArabicCmapFontsDraw renders the fork's `cmap.ass` cases through the libass adapter with its CC0 fonts (`tests/fixtures/libass-fork`): every case draws, and the text in the U+F120 and U+0020 symbol-cmap fonts draws exactly the frame the usual U+F020 layout draws (each glyph shows the bits of the character it stands for). Legacy keeps 0.17.5 (and legacy Linux its distribution's libass) until its own move to the fork, so legacy and the rewrite differ on these fonts until then. Evidence of the fork's frame comparisons: `~/Work/libass-fork-rollout/work/wave-a-prepend/`, the fork's `compare/fork-test/README.md`. |
| H2-output-device | Legacy has no output device choice: every sound went to the system's default output (the audio box through DirectSound's default device on Windows, A4-wasapi-default; GStreamer's default sink on Linux, A4-linux-player). The rewrite's H2 session on Windows 11 hardware ([results](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/blob/evidence/win-hw-20261015/evidence/win-hw-20261015/h2/results.md), 2026-10-15) found both of its paths keeping the device they first opened: a Line audition after the Windows default changed still played on the old device (A10), neither the editor output nor Qt Multimedia playback followed a default change during playback (A9, B6), a WASAPI unplug stopped the sound and showed "Audio player failed." (A11), and video playback went silent on an unplug while the video ran on, with 165 `AUDCLNT_E_DEVICE_INVALIDATED` lines (B7). | Both audio paths, the PortAudio editor output (the audio box and its Line audition) and general playback (Qt Multimedia), follow the system's default output device. When the default changes or the device in use goes away, they reopen on the new default at once, mid-playback too, going on from the frame being heard, with no message; the editor output says "Audio player failed." only after about 3 s with no device to open. Settings > Audio > Audio output > Device offers "System default" (the default, which follows) and the connected devices. The choice is stored by the device's stable id (audio.outputDevice, `QAudioDevice::id`) with its name (audio.outputDeviceName). While the chosen device is not connected both paths play through the default and the choice lists it as "Name (not connected)", so OK keeps it; it applies again when the device returns. The editor output finds the chosen device by name in PortAudio's list for its host API (Windows: WASAPI or DirectSound); Linux PortAudio lists ALSA devices, not the sound server's sinks, so on Linux the choice applies to video playback and the editor output plays through the sound server's default route. The optional DirectShow adapter (W1) keeps DirectShow's default renderer. | User decision 60, 2026-10-16: "Follow + device picker", and "no need to test further after the fix" (no new listening session; the automated evidence closes H2 #125). Validation: `audio_devices_tests` (routing over a fake device list: default change, removal of the device in use, a chosen device missing at start and returning, the picker's entries), `audio_box_player_tests` (ADefaultChangeWhileIdleReopensAtTheNextPlay, ADefaultChangeWhilePlayingGoesOnFromThePosition, LosingTheDeviceInUseGoesOnOnTheDefault, ALostDeviceWaitsForTheNextOne, AChosenDeviceOpensByNameElseTheDefault, TheFillThreadRecoversALostDevice), `options_dialog_tests` TheOutputDeviceIsChosenByIdWithItsName, `general_player_tests` outputDeviceFollowsTheRequest, `shell_tests` audioOutputDeviceFollowsTheSystemAndTheChoice. |
| D3-unobserved-closed | Card #207 requires the native gate on Windows and Linux, monitor removal included, before it closes, and a named obligation that is not observed keeps it open. On Windows real hardware at 3e2b145d3 (branch `evidence/win-hw-20261015`, `d3/results.md`) a live monitor removal failed: the floating Audio panel came back with only its left 173 px on the remaining screen, and once as a window larger than its panel. The fix, 366189ea5 (a floating panel whose monitor goes away comes back whole on the main window's screen at its size), and the whole header as the docking drag on Windows (7812c180f, D3-engine-move) came after that session. Neither was re-observed on real hardware. | D3 #207 closes without the two real-hardware observations: monitor removal after 366189ea5, and docking from right of the title with the whole header as the drag on Windows. Both stay **not observed** on real hardware and are not recorded as a pass. What covers them is the automated and VM evidence: `docking_qualification_tests` floatingPanelComesBackWholeWhenItsScreenGoesAway (offscreen, red without the fix) and wholeFloatingHeaderDocksWhereItDoesNotSplit, which passed on Ubuntu, Fedora, Windows CI and the Winix VM at 0096e2d6d, and the Windows native gate's `pointer` and `snap` steps in the Winix VM at 63e8bc26b (13/13 observed, `floating-free-part-docks` among them). Also left as known: after a live change from 100 to 125 % the floating Line editor keeps its physical size and its content is clipped. A later failure on real hardware reopens this as a defect against D3-engine-move or the monitor-removal fix. | User decision 64, 2026-10-18: "Record the gaps that are left as accepted decisions in the ledger and close all four cards." Validation: the tests and gate runs named here. No new observation was made for this row. |
| H3-uncalibrated-closed | Card #126 asks for calibrated media performance and resource growth on bound reference hosts under the [performance contract](performance.md). At its close: sapphire (Linux, 2026-10-04) and ruby (Windows, decision 60) are bound but uncalibrated. Its only observations are sapphire's contaminated 2026-10-04 runs and ruby's hand-run startup, 50,000-row open and playback memory samples (`evidence/win-hw-20261015`, `h3/results.md`). There is no Windows build of `hikari_core_perf` / `hikari_ui_perf`, no presentation traces (PresentMon/ETW), no pinned G/V/A fixtures on Windows, no A/V calibration hardware (calibrated loopback at ≤5 ms) and none of the three 30-minute wired runs, and no clean idle rerun on sapphire. The seek, frame delivery, overlay, keystroke and composition, waveform and spectrum zoom, cold start and 30-cycle memory workloads were never run. | H3 #126 closes with that work not done. Both hosts stay bound and **uncalibrated**, and `tests/perf/reference-hosts.json` stays empty, so the harness reports no host as calibrated. No gate in [performance.md](performance.md#accepted-thresholds) is passed, failed or qualified by this row: every endpoint stays unmeasured or inconclusive, and the observations stay observations. The thresholds, workloads, fixtures and conditions are unchanged. Closing the card does not change the [cutover policy](proposals/cutover-readiness.md), under which an unavailable mandatory observation still blocks a stable candidate. | User decision 64, 2026-10-18 (as D3-unobserved-closed). Validation: none. This row records a closure without measurement. |
| AV1-linux-pulseaudio | Legacy HikariSub ran on Windows only, with no Linux audio path. The Qt rewrite's general player (Qt Multimedia) and its device list (H2) take Qt 6.11.2's default Linux audio backend, PipeWire, which crashes the application (SIGSEGV in `libpipewire-module-protocol-native.so` on PipeWire's own thread) when the sink a playing stream is on goes away: a chosen device unplugged, or the system default's sink removed under System default (found while observing AV1, reproduced four times, also with AV1's notice compiled out). | On Linux the application starts Qt Multimedia on its PulseAudio backend (`QT_AUDIO_BACKEND=pulseaudio`, served by pipewire-pulse on a PipeWire desktop) unless `QT_AUDIO_BACKEND` is already set, so a user's own choice stays. With it the stream moves to the new default when its sink goes away and the application goes on (AV1's notice says so). Windows and macOS are unchanged; the editor output (PortAudio, ADR 0010) is not affected. | Owner, 2026-10-19 ("Approved migrate to pulseaudio", answering [#238](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/238#issuecomment-6261426990)). Validation: `shell_tests` linuxAudioPrefersPulseAudio; native Linux run with a private PipeWire ([notes](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/blob/evidence/w6-av/evidence/w6-av/linux/results.md)). |
| AV2-cursor-sync | Legacy marked the video's position in the audio box only while the video was Paused (AudioDisplay::DoUpdateImage draws `VideoBox::Tell()` only under `GetState() == Paused`, HikariSub/AudioDisplay.cpp:677-688; A1 keeps it), so a playing video left no cursor in the box, and the box's playback never moved the video. | The audio panel's tool strip has a sync toggle (`audio.syncVideo`, on by default, kept in the profile). While it is on, the audio cursor follows the playing video as a playback cursor (no time beside it) and the view keeps it in sight with UpdateTimer's 50-column rule (without redrawing a view that cannot move); the paused video shows the frame the playing box is at (FrameShownAt, one decode at a time, the newest wanted frame once the last arrived). Off, legacy's behaviour. Which mode plays and owns the clock is unchanged (ADR 0009): the box's play still pauses the video, the video never plays from the box, and the box's own playback cursor wins over the video's while it plays. | Owner, 2026-10-18 (wave-6 decisions, "two-way video/audio cursor sync (default on)"; [AV2 #239](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/239)). Validation: `audio_playback_tests` FollowedCursorKeepsTheVideoPositionInSight, FollowedCursorAtTheStartDoesNotRedrawEachTime; `shell_tests` audioAndVideoCursorsFollowEachOther. Native drift observation on Linux and Windows still required (the card). |
| AV3-dummy-video-audio | Legacy's Audio > "Open blank 2h30m audio" (GLOBAL_OPEN_DUMMY_AUDIO, HikariSubFrame.cpp:301-302, 1077) opened ProviderDummy's 2 h 30 min of 44.1 kHz silence whatever the video, and a dummy video (GLOBAL_OPEN_DUMMY_VIDEO) loaded without touching the audio (Notebook::LoadVideo's dontLoadAudio, HikariSubFrame.cpp:1073). The rewrite's session restore also left a dummy video unresolved (it is no file). | A dummy video always brings its own silent audio, 44.1 kHz mono and as long as the video (its frames times the frame duration of its timecodes), in place of the box's audio, unless a restored tab keeps an audio file of its own. The silence is the video's own, as a file's track is: the session keeps the dummy video alone and restoring it brings both back. "Open blank 2h30m audio" is retired: it is in no menu, has no default binding and no name in the shortcut editor; a stored binding of it is dropped with a notice as the hotkeys are read, and the importer (O3) marks it retired. An older session's blank audio (`dummy-audio:…ln=396900000`) still restores. | Owner, 2026-10-18 (wave-6 decisions, "dummy video brings silent audio"; [AV3 #240](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/240), "Approved departure"). Validation: `audio_display_tests` BlankAudioOfAGivenLength; `video_sources_tests` the dummy's audio frames; `hotkeys_tests` RetiredBlankAudioBindingsAreDropped; `settings_import_tests` RetiredOptionsAndActionsAreDroppedWithANotice, the 243-action round trip; `shell_tests` dummyVideoFromItsDialog; `session_tabs_tests` aDummyVideoRestoresWithItsSilence. |
| WS3-view-arrangements-retired | Legacy's View menu had five arrangements, View all, View video and subtitles, View audio and subtitles, View only video and View only subtitles (GLOBAL_VIEW_ALL..GLOBAL_VIEW_SUBS, 5031-5035; HikariSub/HikariSubFrame.cpp:308-312, OnMenuSelected 849-892), and the Subtitles menu had "Enable / Disable editor" (GLOBAL_EDITOR 5109, default Ctrl-E; HideEditor, HikariSubFrame.cpp:2009-2070), which showed only the video (the player layout), saved per tab as "Editor:" (Notebook.cpp:1350) with EDITOR_ON for the next start. D2 (#201) carried them over the docked Workspace. | Retired. The four layout presets (Editing, Timing, Translation, Typesetting) stay, and the user's saved layouts replace the arrangements: Save layout as… keeps the current Document tab's arrangement under a name, choosing a saved layout applies it to the current tab, Manage layouts… renames and deletes, and Reset this layout returns the tab to the saved layout (or preset) it started from. Saved layouts are global (every tab can use them) and persist across restarts in `layouts.json` beside `layout.json`; they are snapshots (a tab changed afterwards does not change the saved layout); names are unique in any case, at most 64 characters, and a preset's name is refused; an entry this version cannot use is listed disabled with the reason and written back as read, and an unreadable file is kept as `layouts.json.unrestored` with a notice. The presets and saved layouts are listed by View > Layout and by the layout switcher, a self-contained button at the status bar's trailing end until SH1 (#220) moves it into the title bar. The editor is always shown: no player layout, no editor switch and no `workspace.editorOn` (a saved value leaves the INI file). The six commands are in no menu and not in the shortcut editor (no name, no default; Ctrl-E is free); a stored binding of one is dropped as the hotkeys are read, with a notice naming the command and its keys, and the list is written without it (as V3-indexing-retired); the settings importer (O3) marks the bindings and EDITOR_ON Retired with the reason. A Session tab with "Editor: 0" opens with the editor, and every tab is written "Editor: 1" so legacy opens it with the editor too. Video fullscreen's "Open editor" (VideoBox::OpenEditor) stays in the fullscreen context menu with no shortcut: it pauses, selects the Line at the video's time and leaves fullscreen. This supersedes D2-one-editor-switch, D2-all-restores-arrangement, D2-player-video-docked and V5-open-editor-now. | User, 2026-10-18, wave-6 owner feedback on [#213](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/213), card [WS3 #217](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/217); the switcher's place until SH1 (the status bar's trailing end, with View > Layout) and fullscreen's Open editor kept with no shortcut were asked on the card and approved by the owner, 2026-10-19 ([confirmation](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/217#issuecomment-6264684864)). Validation: saved_layouts_tests (theMenusListTheLayouts, saveAndApplyALayout, savedLayoutsAreGlobalAndPersist, resetGoesBackToTheTabsLayout, renameAndDelete, namesAndReplacing, unusableLayoutsAreKept, retiredCommandsAreGone, aPlayerLayoutSessionTabOpensWithTheEditor); hotkeys_tests NamesTableIsHotkeysNaming, DefaultsAreLoadDefault, RetiredViewAndEditorBindingsAreDropped; settings_import_tests RetiredViewArrangementsAndEditorSwitchAreDroppedWithANotice; settings_store_tests withdrawnColourSettingsLeaveTheProfile; shell_tests everyDefaultBindingReachesItsCommand (KB3's audit: legacy's Ctrl-E reaches nothing in each context); the native gate's `layouts` step on Linux and Windows. |

| KB2-space-plays-everywhere | Legacy binds VIDEO_PLAY_PAUSE (Space) in the Video window's accelerator table and hands it to the Grid's (TabPanel::SetAccels); the audio box, the Line editor's controls and the other panels have no Space binding, and a focused button takes Space as a press. The rewrite also left the focus on its Previous frame and Next frame buttons after a click, so Space then stepped a frame back (#243). | Whatever key VIDEO_PLAY_PAUSE has in the Video window (Space by default) plays and pauses from every panel and from a control the mouse focused, before the control's own handling (`src/ui/key_router.h`). | KB1-hover-hotkeys | Legacy AudioDisplay::OnMouseEvent (AudioDisplay.cpp:2024) takes the keyboard focus whenever the pointer moves over the waveform while AUDIO_AUTO_FOCUS is on (the default), and keeps it after the pointer leaves. No other panel reacts to hover. | No panel takes the focus on hover. The hotkey context follows the pointer: over a panel other than the focused one, that panel's bindings (Grid, Line editor, Video, Audio, the Reference tray's Grid bindings) act while the focus stays where it was, and a binding only the focused panel has does not act (Global bindings still do). With the pointer outside the panels the focused panel's bindings act. A text field with focus keeps its typing until the user clicks elsewhere; a control the keyboard moved to keeps its keys. AUDIO_AUTO_FOCUS is folded in as `audio.autoFocus`, "Hotkeys follow the panel under the pointer" (Audio page, on by default, imported from legacy as before): off, hover routes nothing. | Card KB1 #242 (owner, 2026-10-18): "the hotkey context follows the hovered panel for every panel, but a text field keeps typing focus until the user clicks elsewhere"; the card allows retiring or folding `audio.autoFocus`. Validation: `hotkeysFollowTheHoveredPanel` (shell tests) on Linux and Windows. |

Classic arrangement shows them all and hides Play around; Commit stays a split button, and a toggle shown as a button drops its mark from Commit's face. The transport keeps AU1's order; the Back to line chip still shows with Go shown. When the row does not fit, the switches fold into ⋯ first, then the shown extras from the right end, at the top of ⋯; the gear takes room of its own, so at the default 640 px dock Karaoke mode's row (with Auto split) now folds its switches into ⋯ where AU1's fitted, Line mode's still fits. Default arrangement goes back to AU1's row. The choice is one profile setting (`audio.panelControls`); legacy has no such option, so a legacy settings import leaves it as it is. | Owner, 2026-10-21 (wave 6b; mockup and its six defaults approved on [AU2 #268](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/268#issuecomment-6299407262)). | KA2-tap-to-time | Legacy had no tap timing: karaoke syllables were timed by dragging their boundaries on the display (AudioDisplay::OnMouseEvent's karaoke part); Aegisub mainline has none either ([Aegisub#91](https://github.com/Aegisub/Aegisub/issues/91)). | In Karaoke mode a Tap split button (AUDIO_TAP, Y) plays the active Line from 1 s before it to the end of the audio. Hold (the default, `audio.tapHold`): Space down starts the next syllable and up ends it, a release before the next press leaving an empty `\k` syllable (a gap under 30 ms joins the syllable before); Press: each press starts the next syllable and ends the one before. The first press is the Line's start and the last syllable's end its end; taps are the playhead less the calibrated latency (`audio.tapLatency`, wall clock, so `speed` of it in song time), to the nearest 10 ms; syllables keep their tags. Each finished Line is committed as one "Tap timing" step and the next Line follows while playback goes on. Backspace takes back the held and last tapped syllables and plays from 2 s before them (Shift: the Line); Esc stops with the taps kept (Resume, Revert Line), and turning Tap off or a second Esc reverts the unfinished Line. While tapping the other audio hotkeys and plays wait, except Stop. A Line of one syllable asks to be split first. Calibrate latency… plays 16 beeps at 100 BPM (four for practice) and takes the median press delay after outliers. | Owner, 2026-10-21 (wave 6b; mockup approved on [KA2 #277](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/277#issuecomment-6299581272) with all seven defaults). Validation: `karaoke_tap_tests`, `shell_tests` audioTapToTime, audioTapCalibration. |
| KA2-slowed-playback | Legacy played the audio box at its own speed only (DirectSoundPlayer2), and the rewrite's pipeline left variable speed for "separate explicit semantics and tests" ([audio-pipeline](proposals/audio-pipeline.md)). | The speaker popover's Speed (25, 33, 50, 75 or 100%, `audio.speedPercent`, AUDIO_SPEED_DOWN `[` and AUDIO_SPEED_UP `]`) plays every range slowed with the pitch kept, in Line and Karaoke mode; the speaker shows the speed while it is not 100%. SoundTouch 2.4.1 (LGPL-2.1-or-later) stretches the frames on the player's fill thread before any resampling, with its speech tuning (40 ms sequences, 15 ms seek window, 8 ms overlap); at 100% the frames are untouched. Positions, ranges and ends stay in the audio's frames: each heard output frame is `speed` of a source frame, plus the measured lead SoundTouch gives an onset (28 ms of song at 25% to 7 ms at 75%), so the playhead, the video's sync and the taps stand where a sound is heard within 5 ms. A change while playing goes on from the frame being heard. | Owner, 2026-10-21 (wave 6b, card #277; mockup approved on [KA2 #277](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/277#issuecomment-6299581272)). Validation: `audio_box_player_tests` SlowedPlaybackKeepsThePitch, SlowedPositionFindsTheSoundsInTheAudio, ASpeedChangeGoesOnFromTheFrameBeingHeard, SpeedIsClampedAndOneLeavesTheFramesAlone, AClipPlaysAtSpeedOne; `shell_tests` audioSpeedInTheSpeakerPopover. |

| KA5-onset-snap | Legacy snapped only a Line's start and end, to keyframes and other Lines (AudioDisplay::GetBoundarySnap); karaoke syllable boundaries went where the mouse left them, and Aegisub mainline has no onset snapping. | Snap to vocal onsets (Karaoke mode's Tap menu, `audio.onsetSnap`, off by default, since detection is unreliable on smooth singing). On in Karaoke mode, the loaded audio's onsets are found once on a worker thread and kept per audio file (path, track, delay and length) for the session; closing the audio cancels a detection. Detection: the mono mixdown in 40 ms Hann windows every 10 ms, log magnitudes in semitone bands from 150 to 4000 Hz, each band's rise against the loudest of it and its neighbours 20 ms before (SuperFlux, so vibrato within a semitone adds little); peaks over the local mean or median plus 0.12 of the flux's 99th percentile (at least 0.08), 50 ms apart, each taken back to the sharpest doubling of the 10 ms energy from 50 ms before to 30 ms after it; under about -48 dBFS nothing starts. A syllable boundary being dragged (only once the mouse has moved; a click or AUDIO_KARAOKE_MOVE_ON_CLICK places it where clicked) lands on the nearest onset within 100 ms and 16 columns that lies between its neighbours; the right button's later syllables move by the snapped amount, unsnapped. While tapping, each press (a syllable's start, or Press mode's boundary) lands on the nearest onset within 100 ms from the last boundary on; releases stay. Turning it on moves no boundary already placed. | Card #280 (wave 6b, no mockup: a checkable item in the existing Tap menu). Validation: `vocal_onsets_tests`, `audio_karaoke_tests` ADraggedBoundarySnapsToTheNearestOnset, AClickedBoundaryDoesNotSnap, SnappingKeepsBetweenTheNeighbours, TheRightButtonMovesTheLaterOnesByTheSnap; `shell_tests` audioOnsetSnap. |

File > Recently opened subtitles leaves missing files out but no longer drops them, so the menu looks as it did. | WS5-forward-new-tab | Legacy handed a later start's files to the running program (hikarisubApp::OnInit with wxSingleInstanceChecker; on Linux a wxServer socket, on Windows WM_COPYDATA to the window found by class name), which opened them with OpenFiles (hikarisubApp::OnOpen, HikariSubFrame.cpp:1831-1916): one subtitles file went into the current tab after OpenFile's save prompt, one video replaced the current tab's video, and the first of several went into an Untitled tab without a video. On Linux the instance was the user's across sessions (a lock file in the home folder); on Windows the sender gave up silently after about 4 s without the window. | Files a later start hands over open in new Document tabs and never replace a tab's work: one subtitles file as File > Open opens it with "Open subtitles in a new tab", one video (or audio) in a new tab (its same-named subtitles still offered), one keyframes file in a new tab, several in new tabs after the last, sorted and paired as OpenFiles pairs them. Only an untouched empty tab (P6) takes the first. They wait while a review, a modal dialog or the startup session question is open. One that comes during the quit review that goes ahead gets a new start once the window has closed. | Owner, 2026-10-18: card [WS5 #219](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/219) ("A launch with no files activates the running window"). | SH1-custom-title-bar | Legacy's main window kept the window system's frame and title bar, with its own menu bar (`MenuBar`, a `wxWindow` drawing the menus, HikariSub/HikariSubFrame.cpp:143; HikariSub/Menu.h:247) as the window's first row under it. | Where the desktop allows it, the main window draws its own title bar, after MuseScore 4's ([MS1](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/214), docs/research/musescore-shell.md §1 and §7): one 32-pixel row with the application icon (it opens the window system's window menu), the menus, the layout switcher, the window's title and Minimise, Maximise (Restore while maximised) and Close, the Document tabs under it (WS4-tabs-on-top). The window is frameless; its title bar's free parts move it through the window system, a double click maximises, a right click opens the window menu (the compositor's on Wayland, the window manager's on X11 through `_GTK_SHOW_WINDOW_MENU`, the system menu on Windows). On Windows the frame is MuseScore's native one (`WM_NCCALCSIZE`, `WM_NCHITTEST`; `src/ui/window_frame_win.cpp`): Windows keeps the drop shadow, Aero Snap, Win+arrows, the system menu and, over Maximise, Windows 11's snap layouts. On Wayland and X11 a 4-pixel band inside the window's edges resizes it and a 1-pixel `line` boundary marks it while it is not maximised; there is no drawn shadow (it would need a see-through main window, blended over the video on every frame). The buttons use the theme's hover and press, Close's too (no red); on GNOME the same three buttons sit on the right. The title centres on the window when it clears the menus and the buttons by 32 pixels, else in the room between them, elided; under 80 pixels it is not shown, and a still narrower window folds the last menus into More (⋯). Alt alone and F10 put the keyboard on the menus (SH1-f10-menus): the letters underline, Left and Right walk the menus and the switcher, Down, Return or Space opens one, a letter opens its menu, Esc or Alt gives the keyboard back; the letters also underline while Alt is held. Settings › Appearance › Window has "Use system title bar" (`appearance.titleBar`: `auto`, `system`, `custom`): off by default on Windows, KDE Plasma and GNOME, on by default anywhere else and always on a tiling window manager (sway, Hyprland, niri, river, i3 and others, told by the session's names, their own variables and the Wayland globals they advertise, never by the decorations offered), and on wherever the window cannot go frameless. With it on, the window keeps the system's frame and the first row holds the menus and the layout switcher only, with no title. | Card [SH1 #220](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/220) (wave-6 owner feedback on [#213](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/213)); the tiling default is the owner's decision of 2026-10-19 ([comment](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/220#issuecomment-6256489945)); the mockup and its four answers (theme hover on Close, the same buttons on GNOME, no title in the system row, F10) approved by the owner, 2026-10-20 ([approval](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/220#issuecomment-6286788577)). Validation: window_frame_tests (desktopsAreTold, systemTitleBarByDesktopAndSetting, frameIsFramelessWithTheSystemsHints, titleBarPartsAsTheWindowSystemIsTold, altAloneIsATap); shell_tests titleBarHoldsMenusSwitcherTitleAndWindowButtons (the row's order and names, the hit test, window menu, maximise and restore, the edges, the keyboard, a narrow window, system mode, the setting) and documentTabsSitUnderTheMenuBar; the native gate on KWin, mutter, sway and X11 and on Windows (move, snap, resize, the window menu, the snap layouts, mixed DPI, the four themes). |

The status bar no longer holds it. With the system's title bar it stays after the menus in the window's first row. View > Layout lists the same layouts and commands. | Card [SH1 #220](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/220) ("the layout preset switcher" in the title bar), the mockup approved by the owner, 2026-10-20 ([approval](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/220#issuecomment-6286788577)). Validation: `hikari_backends_karaoke_preview_tests` (APreviewIsWhatApplyWritesForTheLine, ANewerRunReplacesOneInFlight, ATemplateThatNeverEndsGivesWayToTheNextEdit, APreviewFitsInAFrame: median about 1.2 ms on `stock-tutorials`), `hikari_application_template_preview_tests` (12, including PreviewRunsOnlyReadFromTheHost, ThePreviewStandsWhereTheSourcesLinesStood, TheSourceIsLeftOutWhenItsTemplatesMakeLines and UnmarkedLinesFoundToBeTheSourcesAreLeftOutToo), `hikari_ui_template_editor_tests` eachEditPreviewsTheCurrentLineWithoutChangingTheDocument and theVideoDrawsThePreviewInPlaceOfTheLinesOwn, `hikari_ui_template_editor_shell_tests` theVideoShowsThePreviewWithABadge. |
| KE2-preview-trust | 2.1.7 and legacy run a Document's template and code Lines (Lua with the user's rights, `_G` reachable from the template environment) only when the user runs the macro. | The Template editor's previews, matching runs and `$` values run a Document's templates only once the user chose to: the Preview button on the panel's notice ("This file's templates run Lua code with your rights. Preview them if you trust the file."), or Apply. The choice covers the template and code Lines there when it was made, as they read then (the Style, which picks the karaoke Lines a template runs on and so whose Actor goes into its `$actor`, the Effect and the text the templater runs) and in the order they stood in (a named template joins its Lines' text in file order, and code Lines run in it), those the panel's New adds and what the panel's own edits make of them (an Effect that takes a Line out of the templates, and the Style and Actor pairing a panel edit gives a `template line karaoke`, included); an Undo back to any of these is still covered. It also covers each karaoke Line's Style and Actor there when it was made (a `template line karaoke`, which the stock templater runs as both, included), as a pair (a comment's values, a Style's name used as an Actor, or a covered Actor under another covered Style are not covered; the Style picks the templates the Actor goes into): 2.1.7 puts them into a template (`$actor`, `$style`) before it runs the template's `!expressions!`, so they are Lua too. A Document without template or code Lines is no exception: its Actor and Style values reach the templates the user writes and the `$` values probe, so the first template written in it asks too (the notice shows only once there is a template). Template or code Lines that arrive or change any other way (a paste, an import, a translation pasted over one, an Effect, text or Style typed in the Line editor, a draft not yet committed included, also when it goes in with a panel edit of the same Line, a Style pasted in with Paste Columns) ask again, so do template and code Lines moved, sorted or deleted into another order, and so does a Line with a Style and Actor not seen together before (pasted, imported or typed, a draft in the Line editor included); a Line copied, split or added with a pairing already there does not. A pairing with a `!` in its Style or Actor is the whole Line instead (whether its expression runs hangs on the Line's text, Effect and times: a blank Line `noblank` passes over), so any change to that Line asks again; and when the file holds Lua (a code Line, a `!` expression in a template, or a karaoke Line's Style or Actor holding a `!`, which a template's `$actor` or `$style` makes an expression) every karaoke Line is chosen with its text, translation and Effect too, its times aside: that Lua can read them (`orgline.text`; `!$actor!` makes any Actor an expression), so any of them changed another way (a text-only Paste Columns, a split, typing in the Line editor) asks again. That Lua reads the file's Styles and Script Info as well (`orgline.styleref`, `meta`), so they are chosen with it: a Style or Script Info property changed since (a Style imported or edited in the Style manager, a script resolution set) asks again too. Templates with no Lua only substitute values into tags, so their karaoke text edits keep the choice. Each choice is one whole state: the template and code Lines in order with the karaoke Lines' pairings, and a Document runs only in a state chosen (with no pairing it lacked), so a template from one choice and an Actor from another never run together. The runs that read a file's own generated Lines (the matching run for a file without KE1's marks, and the run finding their karaoke Lines) run the templates as the file holds them, without the Line editor's draft, so they run only when that state was chosen too: a draft taking Lua out of a template, then Preview, previews without them until the draft goes in. The choice is kept per open Document and load (its DocumentId and generation: a reload from the file asks again) and never saved. Apply stays available, as the Automation menu's run is. Any macro still running when its file is reloaded has its result refused ("The subtitles were reloaded while the macro ran; nothing was changed."): it was made from the old content, and an Apply's completion would otherwise trust the new one. | Codex security review on PR [#321](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/pull/321) (P1: a restored layout with the panel shown would run an opened file's code without a click; P1: rows pasted into a trusted Document would run without a click; P1: a translation imported over a trusted code Line kept its id and ran; P1: an Actor pasted in reached a trusted template's `!expressions!` through `$actor`; P1: a file without templates was trusted at first sight, its Actor values with it, and the `$` values probe ran them once the user wrote a template; P1: a value held only by a comment was trusted for a runnable Line pasted later; P1: a pasted Line paired a trusted Actor with another trusted Style; P1: a template that is also a karaoke Line had its Actor left out; P1: a trusted template moved to another Style kept its trust and took that Style's Actors; P1: a sort reordered a named template's trusted Lines into other Lua; P1: a trusted code Line read a karaoke Line's text, pasted in afterwards, as `orgline.text`; owner chose 2026-10-23 to fingerprint the text, translation and Effect when the templates hold Lua, not the times; P1: a Style imported into a trusted file reached its code through `orgline.styleref`; P1: an Actor holding a `!` expression read an imported Style the same way; Codex code review P1: the matching run ran the file's committed Lua after Preview chose a draft without it); not in the approved mockup, raised with the coordinator on card [#284](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/284). Validation: `hikari_ui_template_editor_tests` aFilesTemplatesRunOnlyOnceTheUserPreviewsIt, applyTrustsTheFile, aFileWithoutTemplatesAsksBeforeItsValuesRun, templatesPastedInAskFirst, aTemplateRewrittenElsewhereAsksFirst, aNewActorOrStyleAsksFirst, onlyRunnableLinesValuesAreTrusted, aNewPairingOfActorAndStyleAsksFirst, aTemplateThatIsAlsoAKaraokeLineHasItsActorTrusted, aTemplatesStyleChangedElsewhereAsksFirst, templatesReorderedElsewhereAskFirst, luaReadingTheLinesTrustsTheirText, luaReadingTheHeadTrustsItsStylesAndInfo, anExpressionInAnActorTrustsTheHeadAndLines, theMatchingRunNeedsTheFilesOwnStateChosen, theLineEditorsDraftIsPreviewed, thePanelAsksBeforeRunningAFilesTemplates; `hikari_ui_template_editor_shell_tests` aFileRunsNoCodeUntilTheUserPreviewsIt, aReloadedFileAsksAgain, anApplyAcrossAReloadIsRefused. |

It stays while the video is paused or stopped, while one of its menus (the context menu, the output device menu, the mode menu, the visual tools menu) is open, and while the pointer is over it. A drag with a button held (a visual tool's gesture) does not wake it, nor does the hover Qt Quick sends a still pointer each time the playing picture changes (a move is a new position in the window; after the pointer leaves the window, to another monitor, its return at the same point is a move). Moves over the tool rail and the file-name strip count too: their controls take the hover from the picture on X11, so they pass their moves on. | AM1-hotkeys-window-retired | Automation > "Open shortcut mapping window" (GLOBAL_AUTOMATION_OPEN_HOTKEYS_WINDOW, id 5039; HikariSubFrame.cpp:355, 937-939, Automation::ShowScriptHotkeysWindow) opened AutomationHotkeysDialog "List of automation shortcuts" (HikariSub/AutomationHotkeysDialog.cpp): every macro with its shortcut, Map hotkey through the "Hotkey mapping" capture, Delete hotkey, and OK/Cancel around the changes. | The window and its command are gone. Each macro's shortcut is set in the Automation panel, on the macro's row (recorded in place: Enter saves, Esc cancels, a taken shortcut asks "Reassign shortcut" with Reassign, Keep both where legacy allowed setting it anyway, and Cancel, which Esc on the question also is), and in Settings → Hotkeys under the "Automation shortcuts" filter, which lists every loaded macro as "Script › Macro", bound or not, and the bindings whose macro is missing. A macro whose legacy name several loaded scripts share (two scripts of one file name, S44-macro-alias's collision) cannot be given a shortcut: the panel's recording says why and saves nothing, the filter does not offer it unbound, and a stored binding of one stays listed with why but takes no keys there (Map hotkey is off; Delete hotkey still removes it), as the binding would run none of them. While Settings → Hotkeys is open its list follows the scripts: a macro registered meanwhile is added, and the row of a macro unloaded meanwhile goes unless it has keys or was given keys there. Both edit the same bindings (the "Script <file>-<n>" lines of the hotkeys map), so stored bindings carry over unchanged. A panel change applies at once (saved and installed), as Shift+click mapping always did; Settings → Hotkeys keeps its OK/Apply. A stored binding of the retired command is dropped as the hotkeys are read, with a notice in the log, as V3-indexing-retired's is. A macro's shortcut that a Global command also uses is named in both places ("Save takes it"; Exit, which the Hotkeys page does not list, too; and the Video window's Play / Pause, which KB2 makes act from every panel); which one runs is unchanged. A macro's keys from the stored bindings or Import legacy hotkeys are read in Qt's modifier order (legacy recorded them as pressed, Alt-Ctrl-K), so the same keys are named as taken whatever order legacy wrote. A shortcut being recorded is the recorder's whichever panel the pointer is over (KB1's key router stands aside for it). Shift+click on a macro in the Automation menu (OnMapHkey, which opened the "Hotkey mapping" capture) opens the panel recording that macro's shortcut, so macros have one recording UI. A shortcut recorded in the panel or given keys in Settings → Hotkeys is a binding made for the macro loaded now, so one listed as missing because its script changed (S44-macro-alias) resolves again once its shortcut is recorded, with new keys or the same; recording the keys a macro has already asks nothing. Set default in Settings → Hotkeys keeps the loaded macros listed, unbound. A stored binding of the retired command is dropped from the Audio list too. Settings Import reports a legacy Hotkeys.txt binding of the retired command as retired, not as a change to import. Macro shortcuts are off while a modal dialog (Settings, for one) is open over a window, which keeps the panel's run status out of reach; so are "Run the last loaded script" (GLOBAL_AUTOMATION_LOAD_LAST_SCRIPT) and "Rerun last macro". The Document scripts' section is "Subtitle scripts": one list for the application, as legacy's Automation::ASSScripts (S4), not the current Document's; the panel offers the Document's Script Info scripts that are not loaded ("This file names scripts: …", Load) as it shows and as the editing target changes while it shows, and loads them only on Load, since loading runs a script's code; the offer names each path without looking at it (a UNC path on Windows would reach its server) and shows it as plain text, its tooltip too (as rich text, markup in a path, an image, would be fetched before Load), and a long field (the value has no limit) names the first eight and counts the rest, its tooltip the first 30 paths, a path given twice offered once, Load skips a missing one silently as legacy's read did, and a Script Info value once read is not offered again (the Automation menu's opening still loads them, as legacy's did, Automation.cpp:1359); their menu offers Unload, which leaves the Script Info naming the script, as legacy's Automation::Remove. | User, 2026-10-19: the AM1 card (#248) removes the window and its command with the bindings migrating unchanged; the panel design approved on the card ("lgtm"), with the recommended answers recorded there (issuecomment-6260166560): Shift+click records in the panel, the retired command's binding is dropped. The card has the name, task, progress (busy until the script sets one), the read-only Document, the log (opened by the first output unless folded), Cancel, and Force stop after 3 seconds (A33-cancel unchanged). A run that applied and logged nothing, or whose script resolution question was declined, leaves nothing, as legacy's window closed; one that applied with a log ("Applied. The panel no longer opens on a run and its run box is gone; the running macro's row carries the busy mark. The card never takes the keyboard: F6 reaches it as a region while it shows and Esc leaves it without cancelling; Automation → "Cancel macro" (no hotkey) cancels while a macro runs. | KA6-lyrics-import | Legacy has no LRC or TTML reader. SubsLoader picks the ASS or SRT reader by extension, and any other file goes through LoadTXT, so an `.lrc` or `.ttml` file opened as plain-text Lines with zero times, one per line of the file (the rewrite's `loadSubtitle` does the same). File > Open listed neither extension, and a dropped or forwarded one went to the video path (`openKindOf`). | An `.lrc` or `.ttml` file is imported: File > Open (with its own "Word-timed lyrics (*.lrc *.ttml)" filter after legacy's two), a drop, a forwarded start, the command line and Recent all make a new Untitled ASS Document. It has the new-Document Script Info and Default Style; the Title comes from `[ti:]`, `ttm:title` or AMLL's `musicName`, each run of its whitespace one space; an empty title leaves the next one, or `musicName`, to name the file, and an empty `[ti:]` keeps the title before it. The tab is named after the file with `.ass`, the Document has unsaved work, and Save asks for a name, offering that one beside the lyrics; the lyrics file is never written. TTML attributes are read by namespace: timing attributes and AMLL's meta fields are unprefixed (`foo:begin` is not a begin), `ttm:role` and `ttm:agent` are in TTML's metadata namespace under any declared prefix (an undeclared `ttm:` too), `xml:id` in XML's, and each attribute keeps only which of those its prefix names, never a copy of the namespace's URI; TTML's elements (`tt`, `head`, `body`, `div`, `p`, `span`, `br`) and head metadata (`title`, `agent`, `name`) are read in TTML's namespaces or in none, so another namespace's `title` or `agent` in the head is not one, nor anything inside another namespace's element (`xml:` too), and another namespace's element inside a title or name adds none of its text, so a `tt` in another namespace is not TTML and another namespace's element in the body is not read, nor its text, times or spans inside a paragraph; the namespace declarations in scope are kept by prefix as elements open and close, so resolving a name is one lookup however many a file declares; a `dur` without a begin of its own counts from the begin its element inherits, and with an `end` too the earlier end wins (TTML2's active duration, the lesser of `dur` and `end - begin`); each element's interval lies within its parent's, as TTML times them: a `<div>`'s, `<p>`'s, span's or word's begin is no earlier than the one it inherits and its end or `dur` stops at the one it inherits (`<div begin="0" end="4"><p begin="3" dur="5">` ends at 4 s; in one real Apple-sourced AMLL file the last syllable, running 0.26 s past the body's `dur`, now stops there), and one wholly outside them is refused at its begin or its end, the log saying that time is outside the times of the element around it; background vocals (`x-bg`) with times of their own keep them, as AMLL writes them over their main Line, starting before it or ending after it (8 such spans in that file), and a `dur` is read, so a bad one refused, even with no begin to count from; a word's span with only a `dur` or an `end` is a timed word, starting at the begin it inherits or at the end of the word before it, whichever is later, and a span around timed spans starts its own text at the begin it inherits too; an entity that can't be read is reported on its own line. After an LRC line's time a bracket is a tag only when its key is a lowercase word (lyrsync's `[class:shhh]`), otherwise it is lyrics (`[Verse: 1]`); spaces between LRC words stay as written on either side of a word stamp. Word-timed lines become karaoke Lines: one `\k` per word in centiseconds, each word lasting until it ends or the next word begins, and an empty `\k` for each pause, so the syllables add up to the Line's duration. Lines without word times become plain Lines. LRC: `[offset:]` applies; a line with several line times is there once per time; Walaoke and `v1:` voice markers become the Actor and hold until the next marker; a line without an end stamp lasts until the next line, the last one 5 s. TTML: `<p>` and `<span>` times are read as absolute, as Apple Music and AMLL TTML Tool write them, and a `<p>` without its own begin or end takes its nearest timed `<div>` or `<body>`'s (a `<body>` without a begin starts at 0, TTML2's document beginning, so `<body dur="2s">` times its paragraphs 0 to 2 s) (a word-timed Line still spans its words; the ancestor's end ends its last word); agents become the Actor (their `ttm:name` when given; an Actor starting with `[` gets a word joiner first, so a name like `[hidden]Singer` stays a name rather than the loader's Line marker); an `x-bg` span is a Line of its own after its main Line (inside a word's span too, and then timed by the nearest timed span around it rather than the paragraph when it has no times of its own), sung by its own agent or else its paragraph's; a timed span without lyrics is no word, and a word without its own end ends with the timed span around it; text in a timed span beside timed spans inside it is a word of its own (going on through untimed elements, `hel<span>lo</span>`, as one word), from that span's begin (or the end of the word before it, if later) to its end, and so is a timed `<p>`'s own text beside its timed words, from the `<p>`'s begin (`<p begin="0">hello <span begin="2">world</span>` sings `hello` from 0 s); such text with no time of its own (beginning no earlier than the word after it, or after the last word with nothing left to time it) joins the word beside it as written (`hello<span>world</span>` from the `<p>`'s own begin stays one word); `x-translation`, `x-roman` and other role spans are not imported. Lyrics text stays literal: `{` and `}` become the fullwidth `｛` and `｝`, and a word joiner (U+2060) follows a backslash that comes before N, n or h or ends a piece of text, so no lyric opens an override block or becomes a line break or hard space; a `<br/>` is still `\N`. A malformed file imports nothing, and the log names the line at fault and what is wrong (for a bad time, the line its value starts on, even in a tag written across lines; the log window shows its messages as plain text, so a quoted `<img>` is never fetched); a clock's minutes or seconds of 60 or more (`[00:99.00]`, `0:99`; only the first part may run past 59), an LRC fraction of more than three digits, a TTML decimal point without digits on both sides (`1.`, `1.s`, `.5s`), times past 100 hours (a `begin` plus `dur` too; a time an `[offset:]` moves, and the last Line's 5 s, stop at 100 hours), an `[offset:]` that is not whole signed milliseconds (a sign alone too; an empty one is no offset), malformed XML (repeated or run-together attributes, the same attribute twice under two prefixes for one namespace, element and attribute names outside XML's Name grammar such as `<$>` or outside the namespaces' QName grammar (an empty prefix or local name, two colons, or a part not starting as an XML name does: `<:tt>`, `a:b:c=`, `a:1=`), a namespace declaration Namespaces in XML forbids (`xml` bound to another namespace, `xmlns` declared, the XML or xmlns namespace bound to another prefix or as the default, an empty `xmlns:p=""`) an element named with the `xmlns` prefix, or a prefix no one declares on an element or attribute (but `ttm:`, which such files leave undeclared), `]]>` in text, `--` in a comment, a DOCTYPE without the root's name, naming another root, with anything after the name but an external id (`SYSTEM "uri"`, `PUBLIC "id" "uri"`), an internal subset in `[ ]` (markup declarations, PIs, comments, parameter-entity references and whitespace only, each declaration by XML's elementdecl, AttlistDecl, EntityDecl or NotationDecl production, its literals by theirs: references in an entity value or attribute default written as `&name;` or a character reference to a character XML allows, no `%name;` inside a declaration, which the internal subset forbids, a public id of PubidChars, element and attribute names in them QNames and entity, notation and processing-instruction target names without a colon, as Namespaces in XML has them; the five predefined entities declared only as themselves, `<` and `&` escaped) and `>`, repeated or after the root, an XML declaration anywhere but the very start or without `version="1.n"` and, in that order, an optional encoding name and `standalone` of yes or no (a `1.n` document is read as XML 1.0, as XML 1.0 section 2.8 has a 1.0 processor do, so `&#1;`, which only XML 1.1 allows, is broken XML), a processing instruction without a target, characters XML forbids such as U+0001, `&#xFFFE;` or `&#x110000;`; a character reference may carry any number of digits, `&#00000065;` is `A`), TTML nested more than 64 elements deep, and a file larger than a lyrics file can be (over 1 MiB, an element tree denser than one node per 8 bytes, or Lines coming to more than 16 times its size or past 10,000 of them (a song has hundreds; each LRC line stamp counts as it is read, so a file of tiny lines stops early), as with one lyric under thousands of line times or a long agent name on every Line; each title, agent name and AMLL `musicName` read from the head counts too, so names nested in names cannot copy a long text over and over, and lyrics text is counted, with what escaping it can grow to, before it is copied) count as malformed. In LRC only a stamp's shape (digits, then a colon and a digit) is a stamp, so `<3`, `<12: tonight>` and `[1st verse]` are lyrics. Like opened subtitles, imported lyrics are offered the video of the same name beside them. TTML whitespace collapses across nested elements unless `xml:space="preserve"` (on the element or an ancestor; `default` switches back) keeps it as written, a tab as a space and a line end as `\N` (kept spaces at the Line's ends become `\h`, as ASS trims a Line, whichever scope kept them, a timed word's leading spaces too); head names (the title, agents) always collapse; a `<br/>` inside a timed span breaks the Line there. An LRC voice marker `v1:` has at most nine digits. Lines end at LF, CRLF or a lone CR in both formats, so a CR-only file's lines and the line at fault are counted right. A TTML time, an LRC stamp's parts and an `[offset:]` carry any number of digits (`000000000000000000001s` is 1 s, `[0000000001:00.00]` one minute). The internal subset is applied as XML 1.0 has a processor that reads no external entity do: a general entity it declares is read where it is referenced (in text with any markup it holds, in an attribute value as text), the first declaration of a name binding, and its attribute-list declarations give defaults and normalize a tokenized type's values; an internal parameter entity's text is read in place of its reference, whole declarations only, while past the reference of an external or undeclared one, never read, declarations are only checked unless the file is standalone (where an undeclared one is malformed); a default is one of the element's attributes, so it may not share an expanded name with another, and a line end written in an entity value is one line feed (a `&#13;` stays a character of its own). An entity naming itself, directly or through others (checked over the whole subset, so a loop the file never uses is malformed too), an entity naming one no declaration gives where the subset is all there is to read (no external subset or parameter-entity reference) or the file is standalone, used or not, an external or undeclared one, one leaving an element open or closing one it did not open, a parameter entity holding part of a declaration or the subset's `]`, and a `<` an entity brings into an attribute value are malformed, reported at the reference; entity text, each attribute default an element takes (its name too) and each attribute declaration an element is checked against share the Lines' budget, and entities nest at most 64 deep (too large past either). In an attribute value each tab and line end is a space. An XML declaration naming an encoding other than UTF-8 is reported as text that is not UTF-8. | Card KA6 [#281](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/281) ("Opening or importing an enhanced LRC (word-timed) or TTML lyrics file creates karaoke Lines with \k syllables from the word timings"), under the wave-6b owner decisions on [#213](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/213) (word-timed LRC/TTML import in scope). Validation: `hikari_core_lyrics_import_tests` (sample files in `tests/fixtures/lyrics`, malformed cases with their lines), `document_files_tests` LyricsOpenAsUntitledImports and MalformedLyricsSayWhereAndChangeNothing, `recent_files_tests` OpenKind, `hikari_ui_open_reload_tests` lyricsOpenAsAnUntitledAssDocument and malformedLyricsNameTheLineAndChangeNothing, on Linux and in Windows MSVC CI. |

The change option is a switch (Set = Insert, Add, Multiply = Multiply and Multiply+, Gradient = the four gradients) with the rest beside it; the values say what they do (Value, By, Start and Per line, From and To) and are typed (Enter) or scrubbed in the inspector bar; a drag sideways that starts on the picture (not the letterbox bars) changes the marked value (◆) with a live preview, Shift a tenth of a step, applied on release as one step; the wheel steps the marked value (By and Add by line number's Per line by one step from 0). The track click and the right-button drag of the sliders are gone with the sliders. The engine is legacy's: every definition, change option and placing writes what legacy wrote for the same values. | KA1-auto-split-language | Legacy's automatic split (AUDIO_KARAOKE_SPLIT_MODE, Karaoke::Split) read every word as romaji by its vowels and n (AUDIO_MERGE_EVERY_N_WITH_SYLLABLE): kana, kanji and Hangul have none of its vowels and stayed one syllable a word, and English words split at their vowels as romaji. | Romaji (Hepburn, macron and circumflex long vowels, composed or with a combining mark; another mark makes the word no romaji) and kana split by mora: a small kana joins the one before, the long vowel mark is a mora, a syllabic n (ん, n, m before b p m) joins the syllable before with AUDIO_MERGE_EVERY_N_WITH_SYLLABLE and always at a word's end; a double consonant (っ, kk, tch) ends the syllable before it, or with the new profile setting `audio.karaokeDoubleConsonantApart` ("Give a double consonant its own syllable", off) is a syllable of its own. Kanji and Hangul (with the compatibility and halfwidth jamo) split per character, a new script starts a syllable. English splits by Liang hyphenation over the shipped hyph_en_US.dic (two letters at least either side); a Line is English when its Latin words that are no romaji outweigh those that are (each word weighing its letters past the second), and in a romaji Line a word that is no romaji is English. Spaces, punctuation and \N \h \n stay with the syllable before as legacy; digits and symbols (legacy's punctuation test takes them in; Auto split tells them apart) open the syllable after them, and a typographic ligature (ﬁ, ﬃ) is a letter of its word. Kanji include the CJK extensions through H, and Latin letters the IPA and Latin Extended-C to G blocks (`ɐ`, `ꞵ`); punctuation and symbols past the BMP are read by their code point. | Owner, card KA1 [#276](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/276) (wave 6b): "romaji and kana split by mora, with options for double consonants and n; English splits by hyphenation". | KA1-lead-in-keeps-syllables | Moving a karaoke Line's start (a left drag of the start, AUDIO_ADD_LEAD_IN in karaoke mode, or a start commit in Line mode) left the \k times as they were, so GetText measured the first syllable from the new start: the first syllable took or lost the difference and every syllable after it played at the wrong time on a later edit ("squashed"). | The syllables stay at their audio times, the \k text written for the start the commit writes (10 ms in ASS, a frame's start in MicroDVD; a later one first cuts the syllables it passes): an earlier start puts the time before them into an empty lead syllable (a new `{\kN}` or the empty one there); a later one cuts the syllables it passes to no length and drops a lead syllable it empties. In Line mode this applies to the text and translation of one selected Line with \k tags (several selected Lines keep their texts). The start's right drag still carries the syllables; either drag moves them by the start the commit writes, not by the dragged time (a TMPlayer start dragged within its second moves no syllable), a lead-in after such a start runs its lead to the start the commit wrote, a letter click splits the syllable's time from that start, the right drag keeps their lengths when the start is off the 10 ms grid (a MicroDVD frame's start), and a drag the window loses moves them, and commits a right drag's carried syllables to the active Line alone, as a release does. After a commit in karaoke mode the selection's start is the start the commit wrote (a 105 ms lead-in from 200 ms in ASS starts the selection at 90, not 95), so the syllables are drawn, played and hit from the start their text is read from. A Line whose last block, never closed, holds a \k tag, or with one override block of more than 16384 \k tags, keeps its text (Codex security: its text is read a piece at a time, and a piece ends only between blocks). | Owner, card KA1 [#276](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/276) (wave 6b): "moving the lead-in no longer squashes the syllables"; owner, 2026-10-21: an empty lead syllable, in Line mode too. For each implementation, add source/fixture hashes, runtime/platform, reproduction steps, old observations, the approved expected result and actual comparison evidence. Keep unexecuted or inconclusive results explicit. The user approved the intended changes from source-backed examples; no legacy runtime characterization was performed during that approval.

An [initial synthetic fixture pack](fixtures/approved-departures/README.md) supplies hashed inputs and explicit comparison/observation fields for the first six document/timing outcomes, plus limited TLMode and precision supplements. Later transaction/lifecycle/import/Grid outcomes need their own production characterization records; the Grid companion supplies only bounded model/browser observations. Legacy and rewrite runtime fields remain unexecuted; input integrity and throwaway demonstrations are not behavioral qualification.

All other candidate dispositions remain open, including Lua undo/export-filter stubs, mutable validation (beyond S4-validation-edits), dialog argument/coercion differences and unlisted parser/conversion/rounding changes. C07 accepts only the atomic rejection outcome above. Upstream Aegisub behavior is not a substitute for a named decision.

It is enabled for an ASS Document with a file, as the check box was, and its status text says why a plain-text Document cannot take the mode (E5-tlmode-needs-script-info). | TM1-original-keeps-tags | Legacy's "Moving tags" (EditBox::SetTextWithTags, HikariSub/EditBox.cpp:1809-1858; AUTO_MOVE_TAGS_FROM_ORIGINAL, off by default) split an untranslated Line's text when the editor showed it with the crosshair tool: its override blocks went to the Translated field (caret after a leading block, the focus there) and the Original kept only the text, so the first change saved the Original without its tags (E5-tag-move-capture). Tags inside the line were left for the translator to retype. | "Tags from original" (the same toggle and setting, on by default): the Original text keeps its tags. The tags the translation needs without the translator's help are carried: every tag before the first visible character plus each line-wide tag (\pos \move \org \fad \fade \clip \iclip \an \a \q) from anywhere in the line, as one leading block, and a whole-line wrapper's closing tags (a \r that ends it included). The Translated field leaves them out and every change writes them back around it, so the translator types only the translation; a change to the Original's carried tags (\an from the alignment buttons included, which go to the Original) carries the new ones to the translation in the same step. Inner runs (formatting that changes between visible characters, found by state against the Style, not by tag names, \fs and \fe as the renderer reads them; each occurrence its own numbered run) are placed by the translator: a strip of numbered chips under the Translated text, one per run with what it changes, filled once placed; Alt+1 to Alt+9 (EDITBOX_PLACE_RUN_1..9, ids 3020-3028), "Place the next run" (EDITBOX_PLACE_NEXT_RUN, 3029, no key) or a click puts a run on the selection, the same selection again takes it off, and Ctrl+B/I/U over a selection place the run of that style. Each run's number marks it in the Original text and its placed text in the Translated text. A run whose text comes back unchanged once, as a whole word, is suggested with a dotted underline: a plain Tab places the suggestions, Escape dismisses them for the Line; there is no word aligner and no online service. Unplaced runs never block a commit and write no empty tag pairs; the Grid marks the Line ("runs not placed", a corner mark on its text) apart from Not confirmed. Brace comments and \N are not carried, nor a run nothing in the Original ends (a \t never reset), which would go on past its words; karaoke Lines get their leading tags without \k, with a notice under the Translated text; drawing and malformed Lines carry nothing (Paste all copies them), and so does a Line whose tags would cost too much to read (a bound on what reading copies, besides the length and tag-count bounds), or any Line of a Document with more than 4096 Styles. Choosing another Style for a Line writes its placed runs again for that Style's runs. Formatting of the translator's own over a placed run (Ctrl+B where the Original has no bold run) goes into the translation's text, the run staying placed. With several Lines selected, an edit to the Original carries each Line's own translation, never the active Line's. Escape and Undo put the runs' numbers back with their text, and a dismissed suggestion holds only for the Original and Style it was read from. A translation whose own leading or trailing tags differ from the Original's is shown whole and left alone. Paste all gives the Original's text with its runs placed. Saved files are unchanged ASS; nothing extra is stored. Any visual tool, not only the crosshair. | Owner, 2026-10-20, card [#232](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/232) ([design](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/232#issuecomment-6287355661), [approval](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/232#issuecomment-6287498458); research in [translation-tag-transfer.md](../research/translation-tag-transfer.md)). | TM1-libass-reading | Tags from original (TM1-original-keeps-tags) read some overrides otherwise than libass, the renderer (#341, from #295's Codex rounds 13-21): a number with no digits kept its spelling (`\fspabc` made a run), `\p1e-3` read as 0.001 (no drawing), `\rName` for a Style the Document lacks counted as a state, a spaced `\ t` or `\ r` was not recognised by its spelling when a run was written, a `\t` animating `\clip` among other tags went to a run whole, adjacent runs with transforms wrote the first run's transforms again, and a tag-button or dialog edit between placed runs kept runs that no longer read back. Legacy's Moving tags read none of these. | Overrides are read as libass reads them (stock 0.17.5 ass_parse.c and ass_strtod.c, as in the altqx fork Hikari ships): a tag's first argument is split as ass_parse_tags splits it (within parentheses up to the first comma; after the name with only its trailing spaces skipped, so `\fs +5` is a size of 5 and `\r Alt` names " Alt"), numbers as `ass_strtod` reads them (no digits, `inf` or `nan` is 0) and whole numbers as `argtoi32` (`\p1e-3` is `\p1`, `\iabc` is `\i0`), `\r` naming a Style the Document lacks is the Line's Style (lookup_style_strict), and a tag is known by its name past spaces after the backslash. A `\t` animating `\clip` or `\iclip` among other tags is split: its clip part goes to the front with the line-wide tags, the rest is read where it stands. Between adjacent runs only the transforms the second adds to the first's are written; transforms that don't go on from the first's come after its closing reset. An edit of a Translated field with placed runs that keeps its text takes out the placements that no longer read back, in text order, as placing a run would refuse them. | Owner: "let's do #341 now" (2026-10-27), within TM1-original-keeps-tags. TagTransfer.ANumberWithNoDigitsIsZero, AnArgumentIsSplitAsLibassSplitsIt, ADrawingScaleIsItsWholeNumberPrefix, AResetToAStyleTheDocumentLacksIsTheLines, ASpacedTransformOpensItsRun, AClipAnimatedAmongOtherTagsGoesToTheFront, AdjacentRunsWriteOnlyTheTransformsTheyAdd, ARunAnEditKeepsFromReadingBackIsNoLongerPlaced; ShellTest::tagsFromOriginalReadsOverridesAsLibassDoes. |

| AD2-dialog-memory | Legacy's script dialog shows the script's values only; nothing a user answered is kept. | The dialog's ⋯ button (the footer's leading edge, before the script's buttons in Tab order) has Last used (the values of the last answer that was not the cancel button or Esc), Presets (load one, Save as preset… with a name, a name in use offering Replace, and Delete preset, which asks first with Cancel the default) and Reset to the script's values. They change only the values shown before a button is pressed, by control name: a value a control no longer takes (another type, a dropdown value not among its items) is skipped, a number clamped. A control showing a value other than the script's has a 2 px accent bar at its leading edge, also in its accessible description; hovering it says the script's value and a click puts that one back. Last answers and presets are kept in the profile per script file and dialog (its controls' classes and names), shared by the script's macros showing the same dialog: last answers in `automation.dialogAnswers`, the 200 keys used longest ago dropped first; presets in `automation.dialogPresets`, kept until deleted. | Owner, 2026-10-21 (Q36, Q37), card [#311](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/311); mockup approved 2026-10-22 with its eight recommendations (issuecomment-6338049672). Validation: `dialog_memory_tests` FillByNameSkipsWhatAControlDoesNotTake; `automation_dialog_tests` presetsAreKeptPerScriptAndDialog, lastAnswersKeepTheNewestTwoHundred, moreMenuFillsAndMarkersPutBack, filledDialogShowsItsValuesAndNotice. |
| AD2-fill-last-answers | Legacy's script dialog opens with the script's values. | An opt-in setting, "Fill in my last answers" (`automation.fillLastAnswers`, off by default; Settings > Automation > Script dialogs), opens each script dialog with its last answers under the safe-prefill rule: a last answer replaces a control's value only when the script sent the same value as last time (compared as the script sent it), so a value the script works out itself, from the selection or its own config, wins; a control new since, or no last answer, keeps the script's value. Off, a script sees exactly Aegisub's dialog. Clear last answers… (asking first) forgets every kept answer and Repeat's record; presets are kept. | Owner, 2026-10-21 (Q36, Q37), card [#311](https://github.com/altqx/hikari/issues/311); mockup approved 2026-10-22 with its eight recommendations (issuecomment-6338049672). The editor is always beside the lists on the selection (no Edit), under its pinned preview, in MuseScore 4's form sections: Name; Font (Family, Size; Font catalog, Emphasis as Bold/Italic/Underline/Strikethrough toggles, Text encoding); Colours and transparency; Shape and position (Border, Shadow, Scale X %, Scale Y %; Angle °, Spacing, Border type, Alignment as a 3 × 3 grid; the margins).
